> AGENT

Found it. On line 775, the installer writes the env to `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/dockyard.env` and then calls `DOCKYARD_ENV="$_dy_env" bash /tmp/dockyard.sh create`.

Inside `dockyard.sh`, `try_load_env()` resolves `LOADED_ENV_FILE` to the absolute path of `DOCKYARD_ENV`, which is `$SANDCASTLE_HOME/etc/dockyard.env`. Then on line 674, it does `cp "$LOADED_ENV_FILE" "${ETC_DIR}/dockyard.env"` — but when `DOCKYARD_ROOT == SANDCASTLE_HOME`, `ETC_DIR` is `$DOCKYARD_ROOT/etc`, so the source and destination are the same file.

The fix should be in `dockyard.sh` — skip the copy if source and destination are the same.

> TOOL

id: toolu_01Jk8VmNy1hnS4z5SpcC8BCa
```
     1→# Installer Build System
     2→
     3→The `installer.sh` is **generated** from `installer.sh.in` template and source files.
     4→
     5→## How It Works
     6→
     7→```
     8→installer.sh.in              # Template with @@TEMPLATE:file@@ markers
     9→  + templates/*.yml          # Static config templates
    10→  + docker-compose.yml.template  # Parameterized compose file
    11→  + /docker/postgres/init-databases.sh  # Source file from repo
    12→  ↓
    13→  installer/build.sh         # Build script
    14→  ↓
    15→installer.sh                 # Generated installer (committed to repo)
    16→```
    17→
    18→## Quick Start
    19→
    20→**Build installer.sh from template:**
    21→```bash
    22→mise run installer:build
    23→```
    24→
    25→**Validate installer.sh is up to date:**
    26→```bash
    27→mise run installer:validate
    28→```
    29→
    30→## File Structure
    31→
    32→```
    33→installer/
    34→  installer.sh.in           # Template with @@TEMPLATE:markers@@
    35→  build.sh                  # Generates installer.sh from template
    36→  templates/                # Template files
    37→    banner.sh               # Login banner for sandboxes
    38→    docker-compose.yml.template  # Parameterized compose file
    39→    docker-logs.sh          # Wrapper script
    40→    dockyard.env.template   # Dockyard config
    41→    sandcastle.env.template # User config template
    42→    traefik-*.yml           # Traefik configs (letsencrypt/selfsigned)
    43→    rails-*.yml             # Initial Rails routes
    44→```
    45→
    46→## Workflow
    47→
    48→### Modifying installer logic
    49→
    50→1. Edit `installer/installer.sh.in`
    51→2. Run `mise run installer:build`
    52→3. Test the generated `installer.sh`
    53→4. Commit both files
    54→
    55→### Modifying embedded files
    56→
    57→**For template files (banner, traefik configs, etc.):**
    58→1. Edit the file in `installer/templates/`
    59→2. Run `mise run installer:build`
    60→3. Commit both the template and generated installer.sh
    61→
    62→**For source files (init-databases.sh):**
    63→1. Edit `/docker/postgres/init-databases.sh`
    64→2. Run `mise run installer:build`
    65→3. Commit both files
    66→
    67→**For docker-compose.yml:**
    68→1. Edit `/docker-compose.yml` for local dev
    69→2. Edit `installer/templates/docker-compose.yml.template` for deployment
    70→3. Key differences:
    71→   - Template uses `${APP_IMAGE}`, `${DATA_MOUNT}`, `${SANDCASTLE_HOME}`
    72→   - Template escapes variables: `\${DB_PASSWORD}` (literal $ in output)
    73→   - Template adds `runtime: runc` to services
    74→4. Run `mise run installer:build`
    75→5. Commit all three files
    76→
    77→## Template Markers
    78→
    79→In `installer.sh.in`, heredoc content is replaced with:
    80→```bash
    81→cat > "$file" <<MARKER
    82→@@TEMPLATE:filename@@
    83→MARKER
    84→```
    85→
    86→The build script replaces `@@TEMPLATE:filename@@` with the file contents.
    87→
    88→Supported formats:
    89→- `@@TEMPLATE:banner.sh@@` - from `installer/templates/banner.sh`
    90→- `@@TEMPLATE:templates/file.yml@@` - explicit path
    91→- `@@TEMPLATE:/docker/postgres/init.sh@@` - from repo root
    92→
    93→## Pre-commit Hook
    94→
    95→Add to `.git/hooks/pre-commit`:
    96→```bash
    97→#!/bin/bash
    98→if ! mise run installer:validate; then
    99→  echo "ERROR: Run 'mise run installer:build' before committing"
   100→  exit 1
   101→fi
   102→```
   103→
   104→## Why This System?
   105→
   106→**Problem:** installer.sh embedded many files as heredocs, which got out of sync with source files (e.g., docker-compose.yml, init-databases.sh).
   107→
   108→**Solution:**
   109→- Single source of truth for each file
   110→- Template-based generation prevents drift
   111→- Validation task catches sync issues
   112→
   113→## Migration Notes
   114→
   115→Old system (pre-refactor):
   116→- `installer.sh` - monolithic file with embedded heredocs
   117→- Manual sync required when changing embedded files
   118→- Frequent drift between dev and deployment configs
   119→
   120→New system:
   121→- `installer.sh.in` - template with markers
   122→- `installer/build.sh` - automatic generation
   123→- `mise run installer:build` - single command to rebuild
   124→
```

     1→class TailscaleController < ApplicationController
     2→  def show
     3→    if Current.user.tailscale_enabled?
     4→      @status = TailscaleManager.new.status(user: Current.user)
     5→    elsif Current.user.tailscale_pending?
     6→      @pending = true
     7→    end
     8→  rescue TailscaleManager::Error => e
     9→    @error = e.message
    10→  end
    11→
    12→  def login
    13→    tag = params[:tailscale_tag].presence
    14→    Rails.cache.write("ts_tag:#{Current.user.id}", tag, expires_in: 10.minutes) if tag
    15→    hostname = params[:tailscale_hostname].presence
    16→    Rails.cache.write("ts_hostname:#{Current.user.id}", hostname, expires_in: 10.minutes) if hostname
    17→    TailscaleManager.new.start_login(user: Current.user)
    18→    redirect_to tailscale_path, status: :see_other
    19→  rescue TailscaleManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    20→    flash[:alert] = e.message
    21→    redirect_to tailscale_path, status: :see_other
    22→  end
    23→
    24→  def connected
    25→    flash[:notice] = "Tailscale is now connected!"
    26→    redirect_to root_path
    27→  end
    28→
    29→  def login_status
    30→    result = TailscaleManager.new.check_login(user: Current.user)
    31→    render json: result
    32→  rescue TailscaleManager::Error => e
    33→    render json: { status: "error", error: e.message }, status: :unprocessable_entity
    34→  end
    35→
    36→  def update_settings
    37→    Current.user.update!(tailscale_auto_connect: params[:auto_connect] == "1")
    38→    redirect_to tailscale_path, notice: "Settings updated"
    39→  end
    40→
    41→  def disable
    42→    TailscaleManager.new.disable(user: Current.user)
    43→    redirect_to tailscale_path, notice: "Tailscale disabled"
    44→  rescue TailscaleManager::Error => e
    45→    redirect_to tailscale_path, alert: e.message
    46→  end
    47→end
    48→
```

     8→  <%# ── Progress steps ── %>
     9→  <div class="space-y-3 mb-6">
    10→    <div id="step-starting" class="flex items-center gap-3 text-sm">
    11→      <span class="step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-blue-500 flex items-center justify-center">
    12→        <svg class="w-3 h-3 text-white animate-spin" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24">
    13→          <circle class="opacity-25" cx="12" cy="12" r="10" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="4"></circle>

    17→      <span class="step-text text-gray-700 font-medium">Starting sidecar container...</span>
    18→    </div>

    21→      <span class="step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-gray-200 flex items-center justify-center">
    22→        <span class="w-1.5 h-1.5 rounded-full bg-gray-400"></span>
    23→      </span>
    24→      <span class="step-text text-gray-400">Waiting for login URL...</span>
    25→    </div>
    26→
    27→    <div id="step-auth" class="flex items-center gap-3 text-sm">
    28→      <span class="step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-gray-200 flex items-center justify-center">
    29→        <span class="w-1.5 h-1.5 rounded-full bg-gray-400"></span>
    30→      </span>
    31→      <span class="step-text text-gray-400">Authenticate with Tailscale</span>
    32→    </div>
    33→  </div>
    34→
    35→  <%# ── Login link (hidden until ready) ── %>
    36→  <div id="login-link" class="hidden mb-4">
    37→    <a id="login-url" href="#" target="_blank" rel="noopener"
    38→       class="inline-block px-4 py-2 bg-blue-600 text-white rounded hover:bg-blue-700 text-sm">
    39→      Open Tailscale Login
    40→    </a>
    41→  </div>
    42→
    43→  <%# ── Error display ── %>
    44→  <div id="login-error" class="hidden mb-4">
    45→    <p class="text-sm text-red-600 bg-red-50 border border-red-200 rounded p-3"></p>
    46→  </div>
    47→
    48→  <div class="flex gap-3">
    49→    <%= button_to "Cancel", disable_tailscale_path, method: :delete,
    50→          class: "px-4 py-2 bg-gray-200 text-gray-700 rounded hover:bg-gray-300 text-sm",
    51→          data: { turbo_submits_with: "Cancelling..." } %>
    52→  </div>
    53→</div>
    54→
    55→<script>
    56→  (function() {
    57→    var currentStatus = "starting";
    58→
    59→    function setStepActive(id) {
    60→      var el = document.getElementById(id);
    61→      if (!el) return;
    62→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").className = "step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-blue-500 flex items-center justify-center";
    63→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").innerHTML = '<svg class="w-3 h-3 text-white animate-spin" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24"><circle class="opacity-25" cx="12" cy="12" r="10" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="4"></circle><path class="opacity-75" fill="currentColor" d="M4 12a8 8 0 018-8V0C5.373 0 0 5.373 0 12h4z"></path></svg>';
    64→      el.querySelector(".step-text").className = "step-text text-gray-700 font-medium";
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    function setStepDone(id) {
    68→      var el = document.getElementById(id);
    69→      if (!el) return;
    70→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").className = "step-icon w-5 h-5 rounded-full bg-green-500 flex items-center justify-center";
    71→      el.querySelector(".step-icon").innerHTML = '<svg class="w-3 h-3 text-white" fill="none" viewBox="0 0 24 24" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="3"><path stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round" d="M5 13l4 4L19 7"></path></svg>';
    72→      el.querySelector(".step-text").className = "step-text text-green-700 font-medium";
    73→    }
    74→
    75→    function updateUI(data) {
    76→      if (data.status === "starting") {
    77→        setStepActive("step-starting");
    78→      } else if (data.status === "waiting_for_url") {
    79→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    80→        setStepActive("step-url");
    81→      } else if (data.status === "login_ready") {
    82→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    83→        setStepDone("step-url");
    84→        setStepActive("step-auth");
    85→        var linkDiv = document.getElementById("login-link");
    86→        var linkEl = document.getElementById("login-url");
    87→        if (linkDiv && linkEl && data.login_url) {
    88→          linkEl.href = data.login_url;
    89→          linkDiv.classList.remove("hidden");
    90→          if (!urlOpened) {
    91→            urlOpened = true;
    92→            window.open(data.login_url, "_blank", "noopener,noreferrer");
    93→          }
    94→        }
    95→      } else if (data.status === "authenticated") {
    96→        setStepDone("step-starting");
    97→        setStepDone("step-url");
    98→        setStepDone("step-auth");
    99→        clearInterval(poll);
   100→        window.location.href = "<%= connected_tailscale_path %>";
   101→      } else if (data.status === "error") {
   102→        clearInterval(poll);
   103→        var errDiv = document.getElementById("login-error");
   104→        if (errDiv) {
   105→          errDiv.querySelector("p").textContent = data.error;
   106→          errDiv.classList.remove("hidden");
   107→        }
   108→      }
   109→      currentStatus = data.status;
   110→    }
   111→
   112→    var urlOpened = false;
   113→
   114→    var poll = setInterval(function() {
   115→      fetch("<%= login_status_tailscale_path %>", { headers: { "Accept": "application/json" } })
   116→        .then(function(r) { return r.json(); })
   117→        .then(updateUI)
   118→        .catch(function() {});
   119→    }, 2000);
   120→  })();
   121→</script>
   122→

     1→class TailscaleManager
     2→  DATA_DIR = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→  TAILSCALE_IMAGE = "tailscale/tailscale:latest"
     4→  TAILSCALE_TAG = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_TAILSCALE_TAG", "").presence
     5→  LOGIN_URL_PATTERN = %r{https://login\.tailscale\.com/\S+}
     6→
     7→  # Stable Tailscale machine name for this server's sidecar containers.
     8→  # Derived from SANDCASTLE_NAME (set in .env) or falls back to the system hostname.
     9→  # Slugified: lowercase, non-alphanumeric runs → "-", leading/trailing "-" stripped.
    10→  TAILSCALE_HOSTNAME = begin
    11→    name = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_NAME", "").presence || Socket.gethostname
    12→    "sc-" + name.downcase.gsub(/[^a-z0-9]+/, "-").gsub(/\A-+|-+\z/, "")
    13→  end
    14→
    15→  class Error < StandardError; end
    16→
    17→  # Restore sidecar from persisted tailscaled.state without re-authentication.
    18→  # Used after reinstall or crash when interactive-login state survives on disk.
    19→  # Note: we intentionally skip checking File.exist? for the state file because
    20→  # the tailscale directory is root-owned (drwx------) and unreadable by the
    21→  # sandcastle process. Containerboot will handle missing state gracefully.
    22→  def restore_from_state(user:)
    23→    raise Error, "Tailscale already active" if user.tailscale_enabled? || user.tailscale_pending?
    24→
    25→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
    26→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
    27→    subnet = subnet_for(user)
    28→
    29→    pull_image
    30→    create_network(network_name, subnet)
    31→    remove_existing_container(container_name)
    32→    # Intentionally skip clear_tailscale_state — we rely on existing saved credentials
    33→    # Use raw tailscaled (same as interactive flow) — NOT containerboot.
    34→    # Containerboot runs `tailscale logout` on SIGTERM, which would expire the
    35→    # saved node key. Raw tailscaled exits cleanly without touching auth state.
    36→    container = create_sidecar(
    37→      name: container_name,
    38→      user: user,
    39→      network: network_name,
    40→      subnet: subnet,
    41→      auth_key: nil
    42→    )
    43→    container.start
    44→
    45→    # Re-advertise routes after tailscaled reconnects — the subnet may have changed
    46→    # after a reinstall (Docker reallocates from the pool). No --reset so we preserve
    47→    # credentials and other settings in the persisted state. Also re-applies the
    48→    # canonical hostname so it reflects the current SANDCASTLE_NAME.
    49→    container.exec([
    50→      "sh", "-c",
    51→      "sleep 3 && tailscale up" \
    52→      " --advertise-routes=#{subnet}" \
    53→      " --accept-routes" \
    54→      " --hostname=#{TAILSCALE_HOSTNAME}" \
    55→      " --timeout=60s &"
    56→    ])
    57→
    58→    user.update!(
    59→      tailscale_state: "enabled",
    60→      tailscale_auto_connect: true,
    61→      tailscale_container_id: container.id,
    62→      tailscale_network: network_name,
    63→      tailscale_subnet: subnet
    64→    )
    65→    user
    66→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    67→    cleanup_on_failure(user)
    68→    raise Error, "Failed to restore Tailscale from state: #{e.message}"
    69→  end
    70→
    71→  # Legacy: one-shot enable with an auth key (still supported for automation)
    72→  def enable(user:, auth_key:)
    73→    raise Error, "Tailscale already active" if user.tailscale_enabled? || user.tailscale_pending?
    74→
    75→    network_name, container = create_and_start_sidecar(user: user, auth_key: auth_key)
    76→
    77→    user.update!(
    78→      tailscale_state: "enabled",
    79→      tailscale_auto_connect: true,
    80→      tailscale_container_id: container.id,
    81→      tailscale_network: network_name
    82→    )
    83→    save_auth_key(user, auth_key)
    84→    user
    85→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    86→    cleanup_on_failure(user)
    87→    raise Error, "Failed to enable Tailscale: #{e.message}"
    88→  end
    89→
    90→  # Phase 1: create and start sidecar, return immediately
    91→  def start_login(user:)
    92→    raise Error, "Tailscale already active" if user.tailscale_enabled?
    93→
    94→    # If pending, clean up the old attempt first
    95→    cleanup_sidecar(user) if user.tailscale_pending?
    96→
    97→    hostname = Rails.cache.read("ts_hostname:#{user.id}")
    98→    network_name, container = create_and_start_sidecar(user: user, auth_key: nil, hostname: hostname)
    99→
   100→    user.update!(
   101→      tailscale_state: "pending",
   102→      tailscale_container_id: container.id,
   103→      tailscale_network: network_name
   104→    )
   105→
   106→    { status: "starting" }
   107→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   108→    cleanup_on_failure(user)
   109→    raise Error, "Failed to start Tailscale login: #{e.message}"
   110→  end
   111→
   112→  # Phase 2: progressive login status check
   113→  # Returns: { status: "starting" | "waiting_for_url" | "login_ready" | "authenticated" | "error" }
   114→  def check_login(user:)
   115→    raise Error, "No pending login" unless user.tailscale_pending?
   116→    raise Error, "Sidecar container not found" if user.tailscale_container_id.blank?
   117→
   118→    container = Docker::Container.get(user.tailscale_container_id)
   119→    running = container.json.dig("State", "Running")
   120→    return { status: "starting", message: "Starting sidecar container..." } unless running
   121→
   122→    # Kick off `tailscale up` in the background if not already done
   123→    cache_key = "ts_login_started:#{user.id}"
   124→    unless Rails.cache.read(cache_key)
   125→      subnet = subnet_for(user)
   126→      tag = Rails.cache.read("ts_tag:#{user.id}") || TAILSCALE_TAG
   127→      tag_flag = tag ? " --advertise-tags=#{tag}" : ""
   128→      container.exec([
   129→        "sh", "-c",
   130→        "tailscale up --reset" \
   131→        " --advertise-routes=#{subnet}" \
   132→        "#{tag_flag}" \
   133→        " --hostname=#{container.json.dig("Config", "Hostname")}" \
   134→        " --timeout=120s &"
   135→      ])
   136→      Rails.cache.write(cache_key, true, expires_in: 5.minutes)
   137→      return { status: "waiting_for_url", message: "Waiting for login URL..." }
   138→    end
   139→
   140→    # Check tailscale status for auth progress
   141→    status_out = container.exec([ "tailscale", "status", "--json" ])
   142→    if status_out.first.any?
   143→      ts_status = JSON.parse(status_out.first.join)
   144→      case ts_status["BackendState"]
   145→      when "Running"
   146→        Rails.cache.delete(cache_key)
   147→        user.update!(tailscale_state: "enabled", tailscale_auto_connect: true)
   148→        ip_out = container.exec([ "tailscale", "ip", "--4" ])
   149→        tailscale_ip = ip_out.first.first&.strip if ip_out.first.any?
   150→        return {
   151→          status: "authenticated",
   152→          tailscale_ip: tailscale_ip,
   153→          hostname: ts_status.dig("Self", "HostName"),
   154→          tailnet: ts_status.dig("MagicDNSSuffix")
   155→        }
   156→      when "NeedsLogin"
   157→        auth_url = ts_status["AuthURL"]
   158→        if auth_url.present?
   159→          return { status: "login_ready", login_url: auth_url, message: "Click the link to authenticate" }
   160→        end
   161→      end
   162→    end
   163→
   164→    { status: "waiting_for_url", message: "Waiting for login URL..." }
   165→  rescue JSON::ParserError
   166→    { status: "waiting_for_url", message: "Waiting for login URL..." }
   167→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   168→    Rails.cache.delete("ts_login_started:#{user.id}")
   169→    user.update!(tailscale_state: "disabled", tailscale_container_id: nil, tailscale_network: nil)
   170→    raise Error, "Sidecar container disappeared"
   171→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   172→    raise Error, "Failed to check login: #{e.message}"
   173→  end
   174→
   175→  def disable(user:)
   176→    raise Error, "Tailscale not active" if user.tailscale_disabled?
   177→
   178→    Rails.cache.delete("ts_login_started:#{user.id}")
   179→
   180→    # Disconnect all sandboxes first
   181→    user.sandboxes.active.where(tailscale: true).find_each do |sandbox|
   182→      disconnect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
   183→    end
   184→
   185→    cleanup_sidecar(user)
   186→
   187→    user.update!(
   188→      tailscale_state: "disabled",
   189→      tailscale_container_id: nil,
   190→      tailscale_network: nil
   191→    )
   192→    delete_auth_key(user)
   193→    user
   194→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   195→    raise Error, "Failed to disable Tailscale: #{e.message}"
   196→  end
   197→
   198→  def status(user:)
   199→    raise Error, "Tailscale not active" unless user.tailscale_enabled? || user.tailscale_pending?
   200→    raise Error, "Sidecar container not found" if user.tailscale_container_id.blank?
   201→
   202→    container = Docker::Container.get(user.tailscale_container_id)
   203→    running = container.json.dig("State", "Running")
   204→
   205→    ts_sandboxes = user.sandboxes.active.where(tailscale: true)
   206→    sandbox_ips = ts_sandboxes.map do |sb|
   207→      ip = sandbox_tailscale_ip(sandbox: sb)
   208→      { name: sb.name, ip: ip }
   209→    end
   210→
   211→    result = {
   212→      running: running,
   213→      container_id: user.tailscale_container_id[0..11],
   214→      network: user.tailscale_network,
   215→      connected_sandboxes: ts_sandboxes.count,
   216→      sandboxes: sandbox_ips
   217→    }
   218→
   219→    if running
   220→      ip_out = container.exec([ "tailscale", "ip", "--4" ])
   221→      result[:tailscale_ip] = ip_out.first.first&.strip if ip_out.first.any?
   222→
   223→      status_out = container.exec([ "tailscale", "status", "--json" ])
   224→      if status_out.first.any?
   225→        begin
   226→          ts_status = JSON.parse(status_out.first.join)
   227→          result[:hostname] = ts_status.dig("Self", "HostName")
   228→          result[:tailnet] = ts_status.dig("MagicDNSSuffix")
   229→          result[:online] = ts_status.dig("Self", "Online")
   230→          tags = ts_status.dig("Self", "Tags")
   231→          result[:tags] = tags if tags.present?
   232→        rescue JSON::ParserError
   233→          # Status not available yet
   234→        end
   235→      end
   236→    else
   237→      # Grab last 20 lines of container logs for debugging
   238→      logs = container.logs(stdout: true, stderr: true, tail: 20)
   239→      result[:logs] = logs.encode("UTF-8", invalid: :replace, undef: :replace).strip if logs.present?
   240→      result[:exit_code] = container.json.dig("State", "ExitCode")
   241→      result[:error_reason] = container.json.dig("State", "Error")
   242→    end
   243→
   244→    result
   245→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   246→    raise Error, "Sidecar container not found — try disabling and re-enabling Tailscale"
   247→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   248→    raise Error, "Failed to get Tailscale status: #{e.message}"
   249→  end
   250→
   251→  def connect_sandbox(sandbox:)
   252→    user = sandbox.user
   253→    raise Error, "Tailscale not enabled for user" unless user.tailscale_enabled?
   254→    raise Error, "Sandbox not running" unless sandbox.container_id.present?
   255→
   256→    network = Docker::Network.get(user.tailscale_network)
   257→    network.connect(sandbox.container_id)
   258→    sandbox.update!(tailscale: true)
   259→    sandbox
   260→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   261→    raise Error, "Failed to connect sandbox to Tailscale: #{e.message}"
   262→  end
   263→
   264→  def disconnect_sandbox(sandbox:)
   265→    user = sandbox.user
   266→    return sandbox unless sandbox.tailscale? && user.tailscale_network.present?
   267→
   268→    if sandbox.container_id.present?
   269→      begin
   270→        network = Docker::Network.get(user.tailscale_network)
   271→        network.disconnect(sandbox.container_id)
   272→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError, Docker::Error::DockerError
   273→        # Network/container already gone or container not connected — desired state reached
   274→      end
   275→    end
   276→
   277→    sandbox.update!(tailscale: false)
   278→    sandbox
   279→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
   280→    raise Error, "Failed to disconnect sandbox from Tailscale: #{e.message}"
   281→  end
   282→
   283→  def sandbox_tailscale_ip(sandbox:)
   284→    user = sandbox.user
   285→    return nil unless sandbox.tailscale? && sandbox.container_id.present? && user.tailscale_network.present?
   286→
   287→    container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   288→    container.json.dig("NetworkSettings", "Networks", user.tailscale_network, "IPAddress")
   289→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   290→    nil
   291→  end
   292→
   293→  def auth_key_path(user)
   294→    File.join(DATA_DIR, "users", user.name, "tailscale", ".auth_key")
   295→  end
   296→
   297→  private
   298→
   299→  def save_auth_key(user, auth_key)
   300→    path = auth_key_path(user)
   301→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(File.dirname(path))
   302→    File.write(path, auth_key)
   303→    File.chmod(0o600, path)
   304→  end
   305→
   306→  def delete_auth_key(user)
   307→    path = auth_key_path(user)
   308→    File.delete(path) if File.exist?(path)
   309→  rescue Errno::ENOENT
   310→    # already gone
   311→  end
   312→
   313→  def create_and_start_sidecar(user:, auth_key:, hostname: nil)
   314→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
   315→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
   316→    subnet = subnet_for(user)
   317→
   318→    pull_image
   319→    create_network(network_name, subnet)
   320→    remove_existing_container(container_name)
   321→    clear_tailscale_state(user)
   322→    container = create_sidecar(
   323→      name: container_name,
   324→      user: user,
   325→      network: network_name,
   326→      subnet: subnet,
   327→      auth_key: auth_key,
   328→      hostname: hostname
   329→    )
   330→    container.start
   331→
   332→    # Persist the subnet so it survives reinstalls (subnet_for reads this first).
   333→    user.update_column(:tailscale_subnet, subnet)
   334→
   335→    [ network_name, container ]
   336→  end
   337→
   338→  def remove_existing_container(name)
   339→    container = Docker::Container.get(name)
   340→    container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   341→    container.delete(force: true)
   342→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   343→    # No existing container
   344→  end
   345→
   346→  # Remove stale tailscaled.state so the daemon starts fresh.
   347→  # Stale state causes tailscaled to immediately set up VPN routes before
   348→  # authentication, which silently drops all normal internet traffic.
   349→  def clear_tailscale_state(user)
   350→    state_file = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/tailscale/tailscaled.state"
   351→    File.delete(state_file) if File.exist?(state_file)
   352→  rescue Errno::ENOENT
   353→    # already gone
   354→  end
   355→
   356→  def cleanup_sidecar(user)
   357→    # Try by stored container ID first, then by name as fallback
   358→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
   359→    [user.tailscale_container_id, container_name].compact.uniq.each do |ref|
   360→      begin
   361→        container = Docker::Container.get(ref)
   362→        container.stop(t: 5) rescue nil
   363→        container.delete(force: true)
   364→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   365→        # Already gone
   366→      end
   367→    end
   368→
   369→    # Clean up network by stored name, then by convention
   370→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
   371→    [user.tailscale_network, network_name].compact.uniq.each do |ref|
   372→      begin
   373→        Docker::Network.get(ref).delete
   374→      rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   375→        # Already gone
   376→      end
   377→    end
   378→  end
   379→
   380→  def cleanup_on_failure(user)
   381→    container_name = "sc-ts-#{user.name}"
   382→    network_name = "sc-ts-net-#{user.name}"
   383→    begin
   384→      Docker::Container.get(container_name).delete(force: true)
   385→    rescue StandardError
   386→      nil
   387→    end
   388→    begin
   389→      Docker::Network.get(network_name).delete
   390→    rescue StandardError
   391→      nil
   392→    end
   393→    user.update!(tailscale_state: "disabled", tailscale_container_id: nil, tailscale_network: nil)
   394→  end
   395→
   396→  def pull_image
   397→    Docker::Image.get(TAILSCALE_IMAGE)
   398→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   399→    Docker::Image.create("fromImage" => TAILSCALE_IMAGE)
   400→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError
   401→    raise Error, "Failed to pull #{TAILSCALE_IMAGE} — check network connectivity"
   402→  end
   403→
   404→  def subnet_for(user)
   405→    # 1. Use the subnet stored in the DB — stable across Docker/reinstalls
   406→    return user.tailscale_subnet if user.tailscale_subnet.present?
   407→
   408→    # 2. If the network already exists on Docker, read its actual subnet
   409→    begin
   410→      network = Docker::Network.get("sc-ts-net-#{user.name}")
   411→      ipam = network.info.dig("IPAM", "Config")
   412→      return ipam.first["Subnet"] if ipam&.first
   413→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   414→      # Network doesn't exist yet — fall through to generate a random /24
   415→    end
   416→
   417→    # 3. Generate a random /24 from the pool (first allocation)
   418→    base = ENV["DOCKYARD_POOL_BASE"]
   419→    if base
   420→      parts = base.split("/").first.split(".").map(&:to_i)
   421→    else
   422→      parts = [ 10, rand(1..254), 0, 0 ]
   423→    end
   424→    parts[2] = rand(1..254)
   425→    "#{parts[0]}.#{parts[1]}.#{parts[2]}.0/24"
   426→  end
   427→
   428→  def create_network(name, subnet)
   429→    Docker::Network.get(name)
   430→  rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   431→    Docker::Network.create(
   432→      name,
   433→      "Driver" => "bridge",
   434→      "IPAM" => {
   435→        "Config" => [ { "Subnet" => subnet } ]
   436→      }
   437→    )
   438→  end
   439→
   440→  def create_sidecar(name:, user:, network:, subnet:, auth_key:, hostname: nil)
   441→    state_dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/tailscale"
   442→    ts_hostname = hostname.presence || TAILSCALE_HOSTNAME
   443→
   444→    config = {
   445→      "Image" => TAILSCALE_IMAGE,
   446→      "name" => name,
   447→      "Hostname" => ts_hostname,
   448→      "HostConfig" => {
   449→        "NetworkMode" => network,
   450→        "CapAdd" => [ "NET_ADMIN", "SYS_MODULE" ],
   451→        "Devices" => [
   452→          { "PathOnHost" => "/dev/net/tun", "PathInContainer" => "/dev/net/tun", "CgroupPermissions" => "rwm" }
   453→        ],
   454→        "Sysctls" => { "net.ipv4.ip_forward" => "1" },
   455→        "Binds" => [ "#{state_dir}:/var/lib/tailscale" ],
   456→        "RestartPolicy" => { "Name" => "unless-stopped" }
   457→      }
   458→    }
   459→
   460→    if auth_key.present?
   461→      # Use containerboot (default entrypoint) with auth key for automated flow
   462→      config["Env"] = [
   463→        "TS_STATE_DIR=/var/lib/tailscale",
   464→        "TS_HOSTNAME=#{ts_hostname}",
   465→        "TS_EXTRA_ARGS=--advertise-routes=#{subnet} --accept-routes#{TAILSCALE_TAG ? " --advertise-tags=#{TAILSCALE_TAG}" : ""}",
   466→        "TS_AUTH_ONCE=true",
   467→        "TS_AUTHKEY=#{auth_key}"
   468→      ]
   469→    else
   470→      # Run tailscaled directly — we manage login via `tailscale up`.
   471→      # Also used for restore_from_state: raw tailscaled exits without running
   472→      # `tailscale logout` on SIGTERM, preserving saved credentials in state file.
   473→      config["Entrypoint"] = [ "tailscaled", "--state=/var/lib/tailscale/tailscaled.state" ]
   474→      config["Cmd"] = []
   475→    end
   476→
   477→    Docker::Container.create(config)
   478→  end
   479→end
   480→
```

     1→class SandboxProvisionJob < ApplicationJob
     2→  queue_as :default
     3→
     4→  def perform(sandbox_id:)
     5→    sandbox = Sandbox.find(sandbox_id)
     6→    return if sandbox.status == "running" # Idempotent
     7→
     8→    sandbox.start_job("creating")
     9→    manager = SandboxManager.new
    10→
    11→    begin
    12→      # Directory creation
    13→      manager.ensure_mount_dirs(sandbox.user, sandbox)
    14→
    15→      # Image pull (can be slow)
    16→      manager.ensure_image(sandbox.image)
    17→
    18→      # Container creation and start
    19→      manager.create_container_and_start(sandbox: sandbox, user: sandbox.user)
    20→
    21→      # Connect to Tailscale if enabled
    22→      if sandbox.tailscale? && sandbox.user.tailscale_enabled?
    23→        TailscaleManager.new.connect_sandbox(sandbox: sandbox)
    24→      end
    25→
    26→      sandbox.update!(status: "running")
    27→      sandbox.finish_job
    28→
    29→    rescue => e
    30→      Rails.logger.error("SandboxProvisionJob failed: #{e.message}\n#{e.backtrace.join("\n")}")
    31→      sandbox.fail_job("Failed to create: #{e.message}")
    32→      sandbox.update!(status: "destroyed")
    33→
    34→      raise # Re-raise for Solid Queue retry
    35→    end
    36→  end
    37→end
    38→

{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/jobs/tailscale_login_job.rb",
  "content": "class TailscaleLoginJob < ApplicationJob\n  queue_as :default\n\n  def perform(user_id:)\n    user = User.find(user_id)\n    return unless user.tailscale_pending?\n    return if user.tailscale_container_id.present?\n\n    TailscaleManager.new.start_login(user: user)\n  rescue TailscaleManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.error(\"TailscaleLoginJob failed for user #{user_id}: #{e.message}\")\n    user.update!(tailscale_state: \"disabled\", tailscale_container_id: nil, tailscale_network: nil)\n    Rails.cache.write(\"ts_login_error:#{user_id}\", e.message, expires_in: 5.minutes)\n  end\nend\n"
}
```

  "old_string": "  def login\n    tag = params[:tailscale_tag].presence\n    Rails.cache.write(\"ts_tag:#{Current.user.id}\", tag, expires_in: 10.minutes) if tag\n    hostname = params[:tailscale_hostname].presence\n    Rails.cache.write(\"ts_hostname:#{Current.user.id}\", hostname, expires_in: 10.minutes) if hostname\n    TailscaleManager.new.start_login(user: Current.user)\n    redirect_to tailscale_path, status: :see_other\n  rescue TailscaleManager::Error, Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    flash[:alert] = e.message\n    redirect_to tailscale_path, status: :see_other\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def login\n    tag = params[:tailscale_tag].presence\n    Rails.cache.write(\"ts_tag:#{Current.user.id}\", tag, expires_in: 10.minutes) if tag\n    hostname = params[:tailscale_hostname].presence\n    Rails.cache.write(\"ts_hostname:#{Current.user.id}\", hostname, expires_in: 10.minutes) if hostname\n\n    Current.user.update!(tailscale_state: \"pending\")\n    TailscaleLoginJob.perform_later(user_id: Current.user.id)\n    redirect_to tailscale_path, status: :see_other\n  end"

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_012P7eNoYn44V3td2ttx6tpS
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/tailscale_controller.rb",
  "old_string": "  def login_status\n    result = TailscaleManager.new.check_login(user: Current.user)\n    render json: result\n  rescue TailscaleManager::Error => e\n    render json: { status: \"error\", error: e.message }, status: :unprocessable_entity\n  end",
  "new_string": "  def login_status\n    user = Current.user\n\n    # Job hasn't created the container yet\n    if user.tailscale_pending? && user.tailscale_container_id.blank?\n      error = Rails.cache.read(\"ts_login_error:#{user.id}\")\n      if error\n        Rails.cache.delete(\"ts_login_error:#{user.id}\")\n        user.update!(tailscale_state: \"disabled\")\n        return render json: { status: \"error\", error: error }, status: :unprocessable_entity\n      end\n      return render json: { status: \"starting\", message: \"Starting sidecar container...\" }\n    end\n\n    result = TailscaleManager.new.check_login(user: user)\n    render json: result\n  rescue TailscaleManager::Error => e\n    render json: { status: \"error\", error: e.message }, status: :unprocessable_entity\n  end"
}

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01RecEqpojiAmc2cpjuFmb89
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/tailscale_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Phase 1: create and start sidecar, return immediately\n  def start_login(user:)\n    raise Error, \"Tailscale already active\" if user.tailscale_enabled?\n\n    # If pending, clean up the old attempt first\n    cleanup_sidecar(user) if user.tailscale_pending?\n\n    hostname = Rails.cache.read(\"ts_hostname:#{user.id}\")\n    network_name, container = create_and_start_sidecar(user: user, auth_key: nil, hostname: hostname)\n\n    user.update!(\n      tailscale_state: \"pending\",\n      tailscale_container_id: container.id,\n      tailscale_network: network_name\n    )\n\n    { status: \"starting\" }\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    cleanup_on_failure(user)\n    raise Error, \"Failed to start Tailscale login: #{e.message}\"\n  end",
  "new_string": "  # Phase 1: create and start sidecar, return immediately.\n  # Called from TailscaleLoginJob — user is already in \"pending\" state.\n  def start_login(user:)\n    raise Error, \"Tailscale already active\" if user.tailscale_enabled?\n\n    # If there's an old container, clean up first\n    cleanup_sidecar(user) if user.tailscale_container_id.present?\n\n    hostname = Rails.cache.read(\"ts_hostname:#{user.id}\")\n    network_name, container = create_and_start_sidecar(user: user, auth_key: nil, hostname: hostname)\n\n    user.update!(\n      tailscale_container_id: container.id,\n      tailscale_network: network_name\n    )\n\n    { status: \"starting\" }\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    cleanup_on_failure(user)\n    raise Error, \"Failed to start Tailscale login: #{e.message}\"\n  end"
}

> AGENT

Everything looks good. Here's a summary of the changes:

### Changes made

1. **`app/jobs/tailscale_login_job.rb`** (new) — Background job that calls `TailscaleManager#start_login`. On failure, writes the error to cache so the polling endpoint can surface it.

2. **`app/controllers/tailscale_controller.rb`** — `login` action no longer blocks on sidecar creation. It sets user to `pending` immediately, enqueues `TailscaleLoginJob`, and redirects to the waiting page. `login_status` handles the "no container yet" state by returning `starting` status, and surfaces job errors from cache.

3. **`app/services/tailscale_manager.rb`** — `start_login` no longer sets `tailscale_state: "pending"` (controller does that now). Only updates `container_id` and `network` after creation.

4. **`app/views/tailscale/_pending.html.erb`** — 
   - Popup opens as a centered 600x700 window instead of a new tab
   - Shows a "login window was opened" notice with fallback button
   - First poll fires immediately on page load (not after 2s delay)

### Flow now

1. User clicks "Connect to Tailscale" → instant redirect to waiting page
2. Background job creates sidecar container
3. Polling shows progress: starting → waiting for URL → login ready (auto-opens popup) → authenticated → redirect to dashboard

     1→class SystemStatus
     2→  DATA_DIR = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→
     4→  def call
     5→    @docker_info_cache = nil
     6→    {
     7→      docker: docker_info,
     8→      sandboxes: sandbox_counts,
     9→      host: host_info
    10→    }
    11→  end
    12→
    13→  private
    14→
    15→  def cached_docker_info
    16→    @docker_info_cache ||= Docker.info
    17→  end
    18→
    19→  def docker_info
    20→    info = cached_docker_info
    21→    {
    22→      version: Docker.version["Version"],
    23→      containers: info["Containers"],
    24→      containers_running: info["ContainersRunning"],
    25→      images: info["Images"],
    26→      runtimes: info["Runtimes"]&.keys
    27→    }
    28→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    29→    { error: e.message }
    30→  end
    31→
    32→  def host_info
    33→    {
    34→      memory: memory_info,
    35→      disk: disk_info,
    36→      load: load_average,
    37→      cpu_count: cpu_count,
    38→      uptime: uptime_info,
    39→      processes: process_count
    40→    }
    41→  end
    42→
    43→  def memory_info
    44→    content = File.read("/proc/meminfo")
    45→    total_kb = extract_meminfo_kb(content, "MemTotal")
    46→    available_kb = extract_meminfo_kb(content, "MemAvailable")
    47→    used_kb = total_kb - available_kb
    48→    {
    49→      total_gb: (total_kb / 1_048_576.0).round(1),
    50→      used_gb: (used_kb / 1_048_576.0).round(1),
    51→      available_gb: (available_kb / 1_048_576.0).round(1),
    52→      percent: ((used_kb.to_f / total_kb) * 100).round(1)
    53→    }
    54→  rescue => e
    55→    { error: e.message }
    56→  end
    57→
    58→  def disk_info
    59→    output = `df -B1 #{DATA_DIR} 2>&1`.lines.last
    60→    fields = output.split
    61→    total = fields[1].to_i
    62→    used = fields[2].to_i
    63→    available = fields[3].to_i
    64→    {
    65→      total_gb: (total / 1_073_741_824.0).round(1),
    66→      used_gb: (used / 1_073_741_824.0).round(1),
    67→      available_gb: (available / 1_073_741_824.0).round(1),
    68→      percent: ((used.to_f / total) * 100).round(1)
    69→    }
    70→  rescue => e
    71→    { error: e.message }
    72→  end
    73→
    74→  def load_average
    75→    parts = File.read("/proc/loadavg").split
    76→    { one: parts[0].to_f, five: parts[1].to_f, fifteen: parts[2].to_f }
    77→  rescue => e
    78→    { error: e.message }
    79→  end
    80→
    81→  def cpu_count
    82→    cached_docker_info["NCPU"]
    83→  rescue => e
    84→    nil
    85→  end
    86→
    87→  def uptime_info
    88→    seconds = File.read("/proc/uptime").split.first.to_f.to_i
    89→    days = seconds / 86400
    90→    hours = (seconds % 86400) / 3600
    91→    minutes = (seconds % 3600) / 60
    92→    parts = []
    93→    parts << "#{days}d" if days > 0
    94→    parts << "#{hours}h" if hours > 0 || days > 0
    95→    parts << "#{minutes}m"
    96→    parts.join(" ")
    97→  rescue => e
    98→    nil
    99→  end
   100→
   101→  def process_count
   102→    field = File.read("/proc/loadavg").split[3] # e.g. "1/234"
   103→    field.split("/").last.to_i
   104→  rescue => e
   105→    nil
   106→  end
   107→
   108→  def extract_meminfo_kb(content, key)
   109→    content[/^#{key}:\s+(\d+)/, 1].to_i
   110→  end
   111→
   112→  def sandbox_counts
   113→    {
   114→      total: Sandbox.active.count,
   115→      running: Sandbox.running.count,
   116→      stopped: Sandbox.active.where(status: "stopped").count,
   117→      pending: Sandbox.where(status: "pending").count
   118→    }
   119→  end
   120→
   121→  def resource_usage
   122→    running = Sandbox.running.where.not(container_id: nil)
   123→    return {} if running.empty?
   124→
   125→    running.map do |sandbox|
   126→      container = Docker::Container.get(sandbox.container_id)
   127→      stats = container.stats(stream: false)
   128→      {
   129→        sandbox: sandbox.full_name,
   130→        cpu_percent: calculate_cpu_percent(stats),
   131→        memory_mb: (stats.dig("memory_stats", "usage") || 0) / 1_048_576.0
   132→      }
   133→    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError
   134→      { sandbox: sandbox.full_name, error: "not_found" }
   135→    end
   136→  end
   137→
   138→  def calculate_cpu_percent(stats)
   139→    cpu_delta = stats.dig("cpu_stats", "cpu_usage", "total_usage").to_f -
   140→                stats.dig("precpu_stats", "cpu_usage", "total_usage").to_f
   141→    system_delta = stats.dig("cpu_stats", "system_cpu_usage").to_f -
   142→                   stats.dig("precpu_stats", "system_cpu_usage").to_f
   143→    num_cpus = stats.dig("cpu_stats", "online_cpus") || 1
   144→
   145→    return 0.0 if system_delta.zero?
   146→    ((cpu_delta / system_delta) * num_cpus * 100.0).round(2)
   147→  end
   148→end
   149→
```

{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/admin/docker_controller.rb",
  "content": "module Admin\n  class DockerController < BaseController\n    INFRA_CONTAINERS = %w[\n      sandcastle-traefik\n      sandcastle-postgres-1\n      sandcastle-web\n      sandcastle-worker\n    ].freeze\n\n    def index\n      authorize :user, :index?\n      @containers = fetch_containers\n    end\n\n    def logs\n      authorize :user, :index?\n      name = params[:id]\n      unless INFRA_CONTAINERS.include?(name)\n        return render json: { error: \"Not an infra container\" }, status: :forbidden\n      end\n\n      tail = (params[:tail] || 200).to_i.clamp(1, 2000)\n      container = Docker::Container.get(name)\n      raw = container.logs(stdout: true, stderr: true, tail: tail, timestamps: true)\n      lines = strip_docker_stream_headers(raw)\n      render json: { container: name, lines: lines }\n    rescue Docker::Error::NotFoundError\n      render json: { error: \"Container not found: #{name}\" }, status: :not_found\n    rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n      render json: { error: e.message }, status: :unprocessable_entity\n    end\n\n    private\n\n    def fetch_containers\n      all = Docker::Container.all(all: true)\n      INFRA_CONTAINERS.map do |name|\n        c = all.find { |ct| ct.info.dig(\"Names\")&.include?(\"/#{name}\") }\n        if c\n          state = c.info[\"State\"]\n          status = c.info[\"Status\"]\n          created = Time.at(c.info[\"Created\"]).utc\n          { name: name, state: state, status: status, created: created, exists: true }\n        else\n          { name: name, exists: false }\n        end\n      end\n    rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n      INFRA_CONTAINERS.map { |name| { name: name, exists: false, error: e.message } }\n    end\n\n    # Docker log streams have 8-byte headers per frame; strip them.\n    def strip_docker_stream_headers(raw)\n      raw.encode(\"UTF-8\", invalid: :replace, undef: :replace)\n         .lines\n         .map { |line| line.sub(/\\A[\\x00-\\x02].{7}/, \"\").rstrip }\n         .reject(&:empty?)\n    end\n  end\nend\n"
}
```

panel.classList.add(\"hidden\");\n      button.textContent = \"Show Logs\";\n    }\n  }\n\n  function reloadLogs(name, tail) {\n    if (!tail) {\n      var select = document.querySelector(\"#logs-\" + name + \" select\");\n      tail = select ? select.value : 200;\n    }\n    loadLogs(name, tail);\n  }\n\n  function loadLogs(name, tail) {\n    var panel = document.getElementById(\"logs-\" + name);\n    var output = panel.querySelector(\".log-output\");\n    var countSpan = panel.querySelector(\".line-count\");\n    output.textContent = \"Loading...\";\n    countSpan.textContent = tail;\n\n    fetch(\"<%= logs_admin_docker_path(':name') %>\".replace(\"%3Aname\", name) + \"?tail=\" + tail, {\n      headers: { \"Accept\": \"application/json\" }\n    })\n      .then(function(r) { data.lines.join(\"\\n\");\n          output.scrollTop = output.scrollHeight;\n        }\n      })\n      .catch(function(e) {\n        output.textContent = \"Failed to fetch logs: \" + e.message;\n      });\n  }\n</script>\n"

> AGENT

Here's a summary of what was added:

### New files
- **`app/controllers/admin/docker_controller.rb`** — Admin controller with `index` (container status overview) and `logs` (JSON endpoint for container logs). Only allows fetching logs from the 4 hardcoded infra containers. Uses `Docker::Container.all` for status and `container.logs` for log retrieval with configurable tail length (clamped to 1–2000).

- **`app/views/admin/docker/index.html.erb`** — Two sections:
  - **Infrastructure Containers** — Cards for each container showing name, state (green/red dot), Docker status text, and a "Show Logs" toggle button. Expanding shows a dark terminal-style log viewer with a line count selector (100/200/500/1000) and refresh button. Logs auto-scroll to bottom.
  - **Docker Daemon** — Grid showing version, API version, container/image counts, storage driver, runtimes, OS/arch, and Docker root dir.

### Modified files
- **`config/routes.rb`** — Added `resources :docker` with `logs` member route under `admin` namespace
- **`app/views/admin/_subnav.html.erb`** — Added "Docker" tab with a monitor icon between Settings and Jobs

     5→    SEVERITY_TO_EMOJI = {
     6→      error: "🔥",
     7→      warning: "⚠️",
     8→      info: "ℹ️"
     9→    }
    10→    SEVERITY_TO_BADGE_CLASSES = {
    11→      error: "bg-red-100 text-red-800",
    12→      warning: "bg-yellow-100 text-yellow-800",
    13→      info: "bg-blue-100 text-blue-800"
    14→    }
    15→    STATUS_TO_EMOJI = {
    16→      resolved: "✅",
    17→      unresolved: "⏳"
    18→    }
    19→    STATUS_TO_BADGE_CLASSES = {
    20→      resolved: "bg-green-100 text-green-800",
    21→      unresolved: "bg-violet-100 text-violet-800"
    22→    }
    23→
    24→    has_many :occurrences, class_name: "SolidErrors::Occurrence", dependent: :destroy
    25→
    26→    validates :exception_class, presence: true
    27→    validates :message, presence: true
    28→    validates :severity, presence: true
    29→
    30→    scope :resolved, -> { where.not(resolved_at: nil) }
    31→    scope :unresolved, -> { where(resolved_at: nil) }
    32→
    33→    def severity_emoji
    34→      SEVERITY_TO_EMOJI[severity.to_sym]
    35→    end
    36→
    37→    def severity_badge_classes
    38→      "px-2 inline-flex text-sm font-semibold rounded-md #{SEVERITY_TO_BADGE_CLASSES[severity.to_sym]}"
    39→    end
    40→
    41→    def status
    42→      resolved? ? :resolved : :unresolved
    43→    end
    44→
    45→    def status_emoji
    46→      STATUS_TO_EMOJI[status]
    47→    end
    48→
    49→    def status_badge_classes
    50→      "px-2 inline-flex text-sm font-semibold rounded-md #{STATUS_TO_BADGE_CLASSES[status]}"
    51→    end
    52→
    53→    def resolved?
    54→      resolved_at.present?
    55→    end
    56→  end
    57→end

   108→

   116→    # Ensure a user's base directory exists and is owned by the current process.
   117→    # On BTRFS, creates a subvolume; on regular filesystems, creates a plain directory.
   118→    # Always safe to call even if the directory already exists.
   119→    def create_user_subvolume(username)
   120→      user_dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{username}"
   121→
   122→      if btrfs?
   123→        if subvolume?(user_dir)
   124→          Rails.logger.debug("User directory is already a BTRFS subvolume: #{user_dir}")
   125→          ensure_owned(user_dir)
   126→          return true
   127→        end
   128→
   129→        if Dir.exist?(user_dir)
   130→          # Exists but not a subvolume (e.g. created by root during install) —
   131→          # skip conversion but still fix ownership so Rails can write into it.
   132→          Rails.logger.info("Fixing ownership of existing user directory: #{user_dir}")
   133→          ensure_owned(user_dir)
   134→          return false
   135→        end
   136→
   137→        create_subvolume(user_dir)
   138→      else
   139→        FileUtils.mkdir_p(user_dir) unless Dir.exist?(user_dir)
   140→        ensure_owned(user_dir)
   141→        false
   142→      end
   143→    end
   144→
   145→    # Create a BTRFS subvolume for a user's data subdirectory
   146→    def create_user_data_subvolume(username, data_path)
   147→      # Ensure parent user directory exists and is owned
   148→      create_user_subvolume(username)
   149→
   150→      data_dir = "#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{username}/data/#{data_path}".chomp("/")
   151→
   152→      if btrfs?
   153→        if subvolume?(data_dir)
   154→          Rails.logger.debug("Data directory is already a BTRFS subvolume: #{data_dir}")
   155→          ensure_owned(data_dir)
   156→          return true
   157→        end
   158→
   159→        if Dir.exist?(data_dir)
   160→          Rails.logger.info("Fixing ownership of existing data directory: #{data_dir}")
   161→          ensure_owned(data_dir)
   162→          return false
   163→        end
   164→
   165→        create_subvolume(data_dir)
   166→      else
   167→        FileUtils.mkdir_p(data_dir) unless Dir.exist?(data_dir)
   168→        ensure_owned(data_dir)
   169→        false
   170→      end
   171→    end

   173→    # Check if a path is a BTRFS subvolume
   174→    def subvolume?(path)
   175→      return false unless Dir.exist?(path)
   176→
   177→      result = system("/usr/bin/sudo /usr/bin/btrfs subvolume show #{path} >/dev/null 2>&1")
   178→      result == true
   179→    rescue StandardError => e
   180→      Rails.logger.warn("BTRFS subvolume check failed for #{path}: #{e.message}")
   181→      false
   182→    end

   184→    private
   185→
   186→    # Create a new BTRFS subvolume
   187→    def create_subvolume(path)
   188→      # Ensure parent directory exists
   189→      parent = File.dirname(path)
   190→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(parent) unless Dir.exist?(parent)
   191→
   192→      # Create subvolume using sudo with full path
   193→      output, status = run_sudo_command("/usr/bin/btrfs subvolume create #{path}")
   194→
   195→      unless status.success?
   196→        raise Error, "Failed to create BTRFS subvolume #{path}: #{output}"
   197→      end
   198→
   199→      ensure_owned(path)
   200→
   201→      Rails.logger.info("Created BTRFS subvolume: #{path}")
   202→      true
   203→    rescue StandardError => e
   204→      Rails.logger.error("Failed to create BTRFS subvolume #{path}: #{e.message}")
   205→      # Fall back to regular directory
   206→      FileUtils.mkdir_p(path) unless Dir.exist?(path)
   207→      false
   208→    end
   209→
   210→    # Ensure the path is owned by the current process user
   211→    def ensure_owned(path)
   212→      return if File.stat(path).uid == Process.uid
   213→
   214→      run_sudo_command("/usr/bin/chown #{Process.uid}:#{Process.gid} #{path}")
   215→    end
   216→
   217→    # Run a command with sudo
   218→    def run_sudo_command(command)
   219→      full_command = "/usr/bin/sudo -n #{command}"
   220→      output = `#{full_command} 2>&1`
   221→      [ output, $? ]
   222→    rescue StandardError => e
   223→      [ e.message, nil ]
   224→    end
   225→  end
   226→end
   227→

    10→# Make sure RUBY_VERSION matches the Ruby version in .ruby-version

    14→FROM docker.io/library/ruby:$RUBY_VERSION-slim AS build

    21→    ln -s /usr/lib/$(uname -m)-linux-gnu/libjemalloc.so.2 /usr/local/lib/libjemalloc.so && \

> AGENT

Now I understand the issue. The Rails container detects BTRFS through the bind mount, enters the BTRFS code path, but can't `sudo` inside the container. So `ensure_owned` silently fails, leaving user directories root-owned, and subsequent `mkdir_p` calls fail with Permission denied.

Let me trace the exact flow:

> TOOL

tool_use Read
id: toolu_01Y19UqrcFAfUGAm3RrQCZrX

  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/btrfs_helper.rb",
  "offset": 105,
  "limit": 25
}
```

> TOOL

   107→      return @is_btrfs if defined?(@is_btrfs)
   108→
   109→      result = system("stat -f -c %T #{path} 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^btrfs$'")
   110→      @is_btrfs = result == true
   111→    rescue StandardError => e
   112→      Rails.logger.warn("BTRFS detection failed: #{e.message}")

> AGENT

The root cause: `btrfs?` returns true inside the container (bind mount preserves filesystem type), but `sudo` is not available. So `ensure_owned` silently fails, and directories stay root-owned.

The fix: `btrfs?` should also verify that `sudo btrfs` is actually usable. If not, BTRFS features are effectively unavailable from inside the container.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01ScrVWDnz4E8Hy24P8cgeS3
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/btrfs_helper.rb",
  "old_string": "    # Check if a path is on a BTRFS filesystem\n    def btrfs?(path = DATA_DIR)\n      return @is_btrfs if defined?(@is_btrfs)\n\n      result = system(\"stat -f -c %T #{path} 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^btrfs$'\")\n      @is_btrfs = result == true\n    rescue StandardError => e\n      Rails.logger.warn(\"BTRFS detection failed: #{e.message}\")\n      @is_btrfs = false\n    end",
  "new_string": "    # Check if a path is on a BTRFS filesystem AND we can run sudo btrfs commands.\n    # Inside containers, the bind mount preserves the BTRFS filesystem type but\n    # sudo is not available, so BTRFS operations would silently fail.\n    def btrfs?(path = DATA_DIR)\n      return @is_btrfs if defined?(@is_btrfs)\n\n      is_btrfs_fs = system(\"stat -f -c %T #{path} 2>/dev/null | grep -q '^btrfs$'\")\n      has_sudo = is_btrfs_fs && system(\"/usr/bin/sudo -n /usr/bin/btrfs --version >/dev/null 2>&1\")\n      @is_btrfs = has_sudo == true\n    rescue StandardError => e\n      Rails.logger.warn(\"BTRFS detection failed: #{e.message}\")\n      @is_btrfs = false\n    end"
}
```

Now I also need to fix `ensure_owned` to actually report failures, since it's also called from the non-BTRFS path. If `sudo chown` fails inside the container, `ensure_owned` should fall back to checking if the directory is at least writable:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01EnS56Ck7iVTuup9D83x3ds
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/btrfs_helper.rb",
  "old_string": "    # Ensure the path is owned by the current process user\n    def ensure_owned(path)\n      return if File.stat(path).uid == Process.uid\n\n      run_sudo_command(\"/usr/bin/chown #{Process.uid}:#{Process.gid} #{path}\")\n    end",
  "new_string": "    # Ensure the path is writable by the current process.\n    # Tries chown via sudo if the directory isn't owned by us.\n    # Falls back to chmod if chown fails (e.g. inside containers without sudo).\n    def ensure_owned(path)\n      return if File.stat(path).uid == Process.uid\n\n      _, status = run_sudo_command(\"/usr/bin/chown #{Process.uid}:#{Process.gid} #{path}\")\n      return if status&.success?\n\n      # chown failed — try chmod as fallback so we can at least write\n      _, status = run_sudo_command(\"/usr/bin/chmod 777 #{path}\")\n      unless status&.success?\n        Rails.logger.warn(\"BtrfsHelper: cannot fix ownership/permissions for #{path}\")\n      end\n    end"
}
```

> AGENT

Now, with the `btrfs?` fix, inside the container the non-BTRFS path will be taken:
```ruby
FileUtils.mkdir_p(user_dir) unless Dir.exist?(user_dir)
ensure_owned(user_dir)
```

If the directory was created by the host as a BTRFS subvolume (root-owned), `FileUtils.mkdir_p` does nothing (dir exists), and `ensure_owned` tries to fix it. But without sudo in the container, even the fallback chmod won't work.

The real remaining issue: when the user dir was created on the host as root by BTRFS, the container can't fix it. The installer's `ensure_dirs` handles this for existing users at install time, but not for the first sandbox creation after a user account is created post-install.

Let me check if there's a user creation hook that should handle this:

> TOOL

> AGENT

No user creation hook. So user directories are only created at sandbox provision time. In the non-BTRFS path (which is what the container will take after the fix), `FileUtils.mkdir_p(user_dir)` will create the directory IF it doesn't exist, and it'll be owned by UID 220568 (the Rails process). That should work fine.

But the issue on the host: if someone already created `/sandcastle/data/users/thies/` as a root-owned BTRFS subvolume on the host, the container Rails process will find it exists but can't write to it.

The safest fix is to also ensure the `ensure_mount_dirs` can create subdirs even when the parent is foreign-owned — by using `docker exec` to fix permissions. But that's complex. The simpler approach: the installer should ensure proper ownership of user directories.

Let me check if this is likely the scenario — the user `thies` is probably the admin user created during install, and the directory was created by the installer's BTRFS subvolume setup:

> AGENT

Now I understand the full picture. The container runs as UID 220568 but has no `sudo`. When user directories were created by a previous install (or by root on BTRFS), the container can't create subdirectories. `prepare_bind_mount` also uses `sudo chmod` which silently fails.

The fix: since the Rails container has Docker socket access, I'll use the Docker API to fix permissions via a helper container when direct operations fail.

> TOOL

  "new_string": "  # Public method for job usage\n  def ensure_mount_dirs(user, sandbox)\n    # Create BTRFS subvolume for user directory if on BTRFS\n    BtrfsHelper.create_user_subvolume(user.name)\n\n    # Ensure user base directory exists and is writable\n    ensure_writable_dir(\"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}\")\n\n    if sandbox.mount_home\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home\"\n      ensure_writable_dir(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n    if sandbox.data_path.present?\n      # Create BTRFS subvolume for data directory if on BTRFS\n      BtrfsHelper.create_user_data_subvolume(user.name, sandbox.data_path)\n\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}\".chomp(\"/\")\n      ensure_writable_dir(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n    if sandbox.persistent_volume && sandbox.volume_path\n      ensure_writable_dir(sandbox.volume_path)\n      prepare_bind_mount(sandbox.volume_path)\n    end\n    # Chrome profile persistence: mount .config/google-chrome separately if not mounting full home\n    if user.chrome_persist_profile? && !sandbox.mount_home\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/chrome-profile\"\n      ensure_writable_dir(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n  rescue Errno::EACCES, Errno::ENOENT => e\n    raise Error, \"Failed to create mount directories: #{e.message}\"\n  end"

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Ensure a bind-mounted directory is world-writable so the sandbox user\n  # (non-root inside the Sysbox container) can write to it. Uses sudo because\n  # after a previous container run the dir may be owned by a Sysbox-remapped\n  # UID that the Rails process cannot chmod.\n  def prepare_bind_mount(path)\n    stat = File.stat(path)\n    system(\"/usr/bin/sudo\", \"-n\", \"/usr/bin/chmod\", \"777\", path) unless stat.mode & 0o777 == 0o777\n  rescue Errno::ENOENT\n    # directory disappeared — race condition, ignore\n  end",
  "new_string": "  # Create directory if needed. If the directory exists but isn't writable\n  # (e.g. root-owned from a previous install or BTRFS subvolume), fix ownership\n  # via a short-lived Docker container (the Rails process has Docker socket\n  # access but no sudo inside its own container).\n  def ensure_writable_dir(path)\n    FileUtils.mkdir_p(path)\n  rescue Errno::EACCES\n    # Parent exists but is not writable — fix ownership via Docker\n    fix_ownership_via_docker(File.dirname(path))\n    FileUtils.mkdir_p(path)\n  end\n\n  # Ensure a bind-mounted directory is world-writable so the sandbox user\n  # (non-root inside the Sysbox container) can write to it. After a previous\n  # container run the dir may be owned by a Sysbox-remapped UID that the\n  # Rails process cannot chmod directly.\n  def prepare_bind_mount(path)\n    stat = File.stat(path)\n    return if stat.mode & 0o777 == 0o777\n\n    # Try sudo first (works on host, not in container)\n    return if system(\"/usr/bin/sudo\", \"-n\", \"/usr/bin/chmod\", \"777\", path)\n\n    # Fall back to Docker\n    fix_permissions_via_docker(path, \"777\")\n  rescue Errno::ENOENT\n    # directory disappeared — race condition, ignore\n  end\n\n  # Use a short-lived Docker container to chown a directory to the Rails\n  # process UID. This is the only reliable way to fix ownership from inside\n  # a non-root container that has Docker socket access.\n  def fix_ownership_via_docker(path)\n    uid = Process.uid\n    gid = Process.gid\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => \"alpine:latest\",\n      \"Cmd\" => [ \"chown\", \"#{uid}:#{gid}\", path ],\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{path}:#{path}\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.error(\"fix_ownership_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n    raise Errno::EACCES, \"Cannot fix ownership of #{path}: #{e.message}\"\n  end\n\n  def fix_permissions_via_docker(path, mode)\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => \"alpine:latest\",\n      \"Cmd\" => [ \"chmod\", mode, path ],\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{path}:#{path}\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.warn(\"fix_permissions_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n  end"
}

> AGENT

Wait — the Docker bind mount approach has an issue. The path inside the container IS the same as the host path (`${DATA_MOUNT}:${DATA_MOUNT}`), but the `alpine` container needs the actual HOST path. Since `DATA_DIR` inside the Rails container equals the host path (same mount point), this should work. But `fix_ownership_via_docker` only mounts the parent dir, not the specific dir. Let me also reconsider — we need to mount the right path.

Actually, looking at the compose: `${DATA_MOUNT}:${DATA_MOUNT}` — so `DATA_DIR` inside the container is the real host path. When we create a new Docker container (via the Dockyard socket), the bind path references the HOST filesystem. So `"Binds" => ["#{path}:#{path}"]` means "mount host's `path` to container's `path`". This is correct since the Rails container's `DATA_DIR` is the actual host path.

But there's a subtlety: `fix_ownership_via_docker` mounts `path` (the parent dir like `/sandcastle/data/users/thies`) and chowns it. The bind mount only gives access to that one directory. But `chown` on the mountpoint inside the alpine container changes the directory's ownership on the host. This should work.

However, Alpine might not be available. Let me use `busybox` which is smaller and more likely to be cached, or even better, use an image that's already pulled:

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Use a short-lived Docker container to chown a directory to the Rails\n  # process UID. This is the only reliable way to fix ownership from inside\n  # a non-root container that has Docker socket access.\n  def fix_ownership_via_docker(path)\n    uid = Process.uid\n    gid = Process.gid\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => \"alpine:latest\",\n      \"Cmd\" => [ \"chown\", \"#{uid}:#{gid}\", path ],\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{path}:#{path}\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.error(\"fix_ownership_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n    raise Errno::EACCES, \"Cannot fix ownership of #{path}: #{e.message}\"\n  end\n\n  def fix_permissions_via_docker(path, mode)\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => \"alpine:latest\",\n      \"Cmd\" => [ \"chmod\", mode, path ],\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{path}:#{path}\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n    container.delete(force: true)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.warn(\"fix_permissions_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n  end",
  "new_string": "  BUSYBOX_IMAGE = \"busybox:latest\"\n\n  # Use a short-lived Docker container to chown a directory to the Rails\n  # process UID. This is the only reliable way to fix ownership from inside\n  # a non-root container that has Docker socket access.\n  def fix_ownership_via_docker(path)\n    uid = Process.uid\n    gid = Process.gid\n    run_docker_fix([ \"chown\", \"#{uid}:#{gid}\", \"/target\" ], path)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.error(\"fix_ownership_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n    raise Errno::EACCES, \"Cannot fix ownership of #{path}: #{e.message}\"\n  end\n\n  def fix_permissions_via_docker(path, mode)\n    run_docker_fix([ \"chmod\", mode, \"/target\" ], path)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.warn(\"fix_permissions_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n  end\n\n  def run_docker_fix(cmd, host_path)\n    Docker::Image.get(BUSYBOX_IMAGE) rescue Docker::Image.create(\"fromImage\" => BUSYBOX_IMAGE)\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => BUSYBOX_IMAGE,\n      \"Cmd\" => cmd,\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{host_path}:/target\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n  ensure\n    container&.delete(force: true) rescue nil\n  end"
}

> AGENT

Tests just check that `ensure_mount_dirs` is called, not its internals. They should still pass.

Here's a summary of the three fixes:

### Root cause
The Rails container runs as UID 220568 without `sudo`. Multiple code paths (`BtrfsHelper.ensure_owned`, `prepare_bind_mount`) relied on `sudo` which silently failed, leaving directories root-owned from previous installs.

### Fixes

1. **`app/services/btrfs_helper.rb` — `btrfs?` method**: Now checks that `sudo btrfs` is actually usable, not just that the filesystem is BTRFS. Inside containers where `sudo` isn't available, this correctly returns `false`, taking the simpler non-BTRFS code path. Also improved `ensure_owned` to try `chmod` as fallback and log warnings on failure.

2. **`app/services/sandbox_manager.rb` — `ensure_writable_dir`**: New method replaces raw `FileUtils.mkdir_p`. If `mkdir_p` fails with EACCES (parent dir is root-owned), it uses a short-lived `busybox` Docker container to `chown` the parent directory to the Rails process UID, then retries.

3. **`app/services/sandbox_manager.rb` — `prepare_bind_mount`**: Now falls back to a Docker container for `chmod 777` when `sudo` isn't available, using the same `run_docker_fix` helper.

> DEVELOPER

is this a sandcastle or dockyard issue?

> AGENT

It's a **Sandcastle issue**. Dockyard is just the Docker + Sysbox runtime — it manages the daemon, networking, and iptables. It has no involvement with user data directories.

The problem is in Sandcastle's own stack:

1. **Installer** — `ensure_dirs` chowns existing user dirs at install time, but if the user dir was created later (or by a previous install as root/BTRFS subvolume), nothing fixes it
2. **Rails app** — `BtrfsHelper.btrfs?` detected BTRFS through the bind mount but couldn't actually do anything about it (no `sudo` in container), and `ensure_owned` silently failed

So the Docker-based fix I added is overkill for what's really a simpler problem. Let me reconsider — the cleaner fix is just in the **installer**: ensure user dirs are properly owned on every install, and in the Rails app: don't pretend we can do BTRFS ops when we can't.

Want me to simplify? The `btrfs?` fix and the `ensure_owned` warning are the right app-side fixes. The Docker container fallback in `ensure_writable_dir`/`prepare_bind_mount` could be replaced by just making the **installer's `ensure_dirs`** more robust (also chowning nested subdirectories like `home/`, `chrome-profile/`, `data/`).

  "new_string": "  # Public method for job usage\n  def ensure_mount_dirs(user, sandbox)\n    # Create BTRFS subvolume for user directory if on BTRFS\n    BtrfsHelper.create_user_subvolume(user.name)\n\n    if sandbox.mount_home\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/home\"\n      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n    if sandbox.data_path.present?\n      # Create BTRFS subvolume for data directory if on BTRFS\n      BtrfsHelper.create_user_data_subvolume(user.name, sandbox.data_path)\n\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/data/#{sandbox.data_path}\".chomp(\"/\")\n      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n    if sandbox.persistent_volume && sandbox.volume_path\n      FileUtils.mkdir_p(sandbox.volume_path)\n      prepare_bind_mount(sandbox.volume_path)\n    end\n    # Chrome profile persistence: mount .config/google-chrome separately if not mounting full home\n    if user.chrome_persist_profile? && !sandbox.mount_home\n      dir = \"#{DATA_DIR}/users/#{user.name}/chrome-profile\"\n      FileUtils.mkdir_p(dir)\n      prepare_bind_mount(dir)\n    end\n  rescue Errno::EACCES, Errno::ENOENT => e\n    raise Error, \"Failed to create mount directories: #{e.message}\"\n  end"

```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "  # Create directory if needed. If the directory exists but isn't writable\n  # (e.g. root-owned from a previous install or BTRFS subvolume), fix ownership\n  # via a short-lived Docker container (the Rails process has Docker socket\n  # access but no sudo inside its own container).\n  def ensure_writable_dir(path)\n    FileUtils.mkdir_p(path)\n  rescue Errno::EACCES\n    # Parent exists but is not writable — fix ownership via Docker\n    fix_ownership_via_docker(File.dirname(path))\n    FileUtils.mkdir_p(path)\n  end\n\n  # Ensure a bind-mounted directory is world-writable so the sandbox user\n  # (non-root inside the Sysbox container) can write to it. After a previous\n  # container run the dir may be owned by a Sysbox-remapped UID that the\n  # Rails process cannot chmod directly.\n  def prepare_bind_mount(path)\n    stat = File.stat(path)\n    return if stat.mode & 0o777 == 0o777\n\n    # Try sudo first (works on host, not in container)\n    return if system(\"/usr/bin/sudo\", \"-n\", \"/usr/bin/chmod\", \"777\", path)\n\n    # Fall back to Docker\n    fix_permissions_via_docker(path, \"777\")\n  rescue Errno::ENOENT\n    # directory disappeared — race condition, ignore\n  end\n\n  BUSYBOX_IMAGE = \"busybox:latest\"\n\n  # Use a short-lived Docker container to chown a directory to the Rails\n  # process UID. This is the only reliable way to fix ownership from inside\n  # a non-root container that has Docker socket access.\n  def fix_ownership_via_docker(path)\n    uid = Process.uid\n    gid = Process.gid\n    run_docker_fix([ \"chown\", \"#{uid}:#{gid}\", \"/target\" ], path)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.error(\"fix_ownership_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n    raise Errno::EACCES, \"Cannot fix ownership of #{path}: #{e.message}\"\n  end\n\n  def fix_permissions_via_docker(path, mode)\n    run_docker_fix([ \"chmod\", mode, \"/target\" ], path)\n  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e\n    Rails.logger.warn(\"fix_permissions_via_docker(#{path}) failed: #{e.message}\")\n  end\n\n  def run_docker_fix(cmd, host_path)\n    Docker::Image.get(BUSYBOX_IMAGE) rescue Docker::Image.create(\"fromImage\" => BUSYBOX_IMAGE)\n    container = Docker::Container.create(\n      \"Image\" => BUSYBOX_IMAGE,\n      \"Cmd\" => cmd,\n      \"HostConfig\" => { \"Binds\" => [ \"#{host_path}:/target\" ] }\n    )\n    container.start\n    container.wait(10)\n  ensure\n    container&.delete(force: true) rescue nil\n  end",
  "new_string": "  # Ensure a bind-mounted directory is world-writable so the sandbox user\n  # (non-root inside the Sysbox container) can write to it. Uses sudo because\n  # after a previous container run the dir may be owned by a Sysbox-remapped\n  # UID that the Rails process cannot chmod.\n  def prepare_bind_mount(path)\n    stat = File.stat(path)\n    system(\"/usr/bin/sudo\", \"-n\", \"/usr/bin/chmod\", \"777\", path) unless stat.mode & 0o777 == 0o777\n  rescue Errno::ENOENT\n    # directory disappeared — race condition, ignore\n  end"
}

> AGENT

The issue: line 318 only chowns the user directory itself (`/data/users/thies/`), not its subdirectories like `chrome-profile/`. And it misses `chrome-profile` in the chmod loop on line 320-324. Fix both:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01Dk5mqirqRPR3gwZmz4cDhD
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/installer/installer.sh.in",
  "old_string": "  # Per-user dirs: own the user-level parent, then chmod 777 the bind-mount\n  # targets so Sysbox-mapped root can write to them.\n  for d in \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*; do\n    [ -d \"$d\" ] && chown \"${SANDCASTLE_UID}:${SANDCASTLE_GID}\" \"$d\"\n  done\n  for d in \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*/home \\\n           \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*/data \\\n           \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/sandboxes/*/vol; do\n    [ -d \"$d\" ] && chmod 777 \"$d\"\n  done",
  "new_string": "  # Per-user dirs: own the user-level parent and all direct children so the\n  # Rails container (UID $SANDCASTLE_UID) can create subdirectories.\n  # Then chmod 777 the bind-mount targets so Sysbox-mapped root can write.\n  for d in \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*; do\n    if [ -d \"$d\" ]; then\n      chown \"${SANDCASTLE_UID}:${SANDCASTLE_GID}\" \"$d\"\n      # Also fix any direct children (home, data, chrome-profile, tailscale, etc.)\n      find \"$d\" -maxdepth 1 -mindepth 1 -type d \\\n        -exec chown \"${SANDCASTLE_UID}:${SANDCASTLE_GID}\" {} +\n    fi\n  done\n  for d in \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*/home \\\n           \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*/data \\\n           \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/users/*/chrome-profile \\\n           \"$SANDCASTLE_HOME\"/data/sandboxes/*/vol; do\n    [ -d \"$d\" ] && chmod 777 \"$d\"\n  done"
}

  "new_string": "    # Ensure the path is owned by the current process user\n    def ensure_owned(path)\n      return if File.stat(path).uid == Process.uid\n\n      run_sudo_command(\"/usr/bin/chown #{Process.uid}:#{Process.gid} #{path}\")\n    end"

> AGENT

Two clean fixes:

1. **`app/services/btrfs_helper.rb`** — `btrfs?` now verifies `sudo btrfs` actually works, so the container takes the plain `mkdir_p` path instead of the broken BTRFS path.

2. **`installer/installer.sh.in`** — `ensure_dirs` now chowns all direct children of each user directory (not just the user dir itself), and adds `chrome-profile` to the chmod loop. This runs as root on the host during install, fixing ownership for the container process.
