```
     1→class VncManager
     2→  DATA_DIR    = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_DATA_DIR", "/data")
     3→  DYNAMIC_DIR = File.join(DATA_DIR, "traefik", "dynamic")
     4→
     5→  class Error < StandardError; end
     6→
     7→  # Opens a web-based VNC session for the given sandbox.
     8→  # Returns the URL path to the noVNC session.
     9→  # websockify runs inside the sandbox container (started by entrypoint.sh),
    10→  # so we only need to connect the sandbox to the Traefik network and write
    11→  # the Traefik routing config.
    12→  def open(sandbox:)
    13→    raise Error, "Sandbox is not running" unless sandbox.status == "running"
    14→
    15→    write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    16→    vnc_url(sandbox)
    17→  end
    18→
    19→  # Closes the web VNC session for the given sandbox.
    20→  def close(sandbox:)
    21→    delete_traefik_config(sandbox)
    22→  rescue Docker::Error::DockerError => e
    23→    Rails.logger.error("VncManager: close failed for #{sandbox.full_name}: #{e.message}")
    24→  end
    25→
    26→  # Returns true if the sandbox's websockify process is accepting connections.
    27→  # Requires the sandbox to already be connected to the sandcastle-web network
    28→  # (i.e., after open has been called).
    29→  def active?(sandbox:)
    30→    return false unless File.exist?(traefik_config_path(sandbox))
    31→
    32→    TCPSocket.new(sandbox.full_name, 6080).close
    33→    true
    34→  rescue Errno::ECONNREFUSED, Errno::ETIMEDOUT, SocketError
    35→    false
    36→  end
    37→
    38→  # Removes orphaned Traefik VNC configs whose sandbox is gone or not running.
    39→  def cleanup_orphaned
    40→    Dir.glob(File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "vnc-*.yml")).each do |path|
    41→      id = path.match(/vnc-(\d+)\.yml/)&.[](1)&.to_i
    42→      next unless id
    43→
    44→      sandbox = Sandbox.find_by(id: id)
    45→      if sandbox.nil? || sandbox.status != "running"
    46→        File.delete(path)
    47→        Rails.logger.info("VncManager: removed orphaned Traefik config #{File.basename(path)}")
    48→      end
    49→    end
    50→  rescue => e
    51→    Rails.logger.error("VncManager: orphan cleanup failed: #{e.message}")
    52→  end
    53→
    54→  def prepare_traefik_config(sandbox)
    55→    write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    56→  end
    57→
    58→  def vnc_url(sandbox)
    59→    id = sandbox.id
    60→    "/novnc/vnc.html?path=/vnc/#{id}/websockify&autoconnect=true"
    61→  end
    62→
    63→  private
    64→
    65→  def traefik_config_path(sandbox)
    66→    File.join(DYNAMIC_DIR, "vnc-#{sandbox.id}.yml")
    67→  end
    68→
    69→  def write_traefik_config(sandbox)
    70→    FileUtils.mkdir_p(DYNAMIC_DIR)
    71→    config_path = traefik_config_path(sandbox)
    72→    return false if File.exist?(config_path)
    73→
    74→    host = ENV.fetch("SANDCASTLE_HOST", "localhost")
    75→    id = sandbox.id
    76→    sandbox_name = sandbox.full_name
    77→
    78→    base_rule = if ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"] == "selfsigned"
    79→      "HostRegexp(`.+`) && PathPrefix(`/vnc/#{id}/websockify`)"
    80→    else
    81→      "Host(`#{host}`) && PathPrefix(`/vnc/#{id}/websockify`)"
    82→    end
    83→
    84→    # Route only the WebSocket path /vnc/{id}/websockify to websockify-go on port 6080.
    85→    # noVNC static files (vnc.html, core/, etc.) are served from Rails public/novnc/.
    86→    # stripPrefix removes /vnc/{id} so websockify-go receives /websockify.
    87→    config = {
    88→      "http" => {
    89→        "routers" => {
    90→          "vnc-#{id}" => {
    91→            "rule" => base_rule,
    92→            "service" => "vnc-#{id}",
    93→            "entryPoints" => [ "websecure" ],
    94→            "tls" => tls_config,
    95→            "middlewares" => [ "vnc-auth-#{id}", "vnc-stripprefix-#{id}" ],
    96→            "priority" => 100
    97→          }
    98→        },
    99→        "middlewares" => {
   100→          "vnc-auth-#{id}" => {
   101→            "forwardAuth" => {
   102→              "address" => "http://sandcastle-web:80/vnc/auth",
   103→              "trustForwardHeader" => true
   104→            }
   105→          },
   106→          "vnc-stripprefix-#{id}" => {
   107→            "stripPrefix" => {
   108→              "prefixes" => [ "/vnc/#{id}" ]
   109→            }
   110→          }
   111→        },
   112→        "services" => {
   113→          "vnc-#{id}" => {
   114→            "loadBalancer" => {
   115→              "servers" => [ { "url" => "http://#{sandbox_name}:6080" } ]
   116→            }
   117→          }
   118→        }
   119→      }
   120→    }
   121→
   122→    File.write(config_path, config.to_yaml)
   123→    true
   124→  end
   125→
   126→  def tls_config
   127→    case ENV["SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE"]
   128→    when "selfsigned", "mkcert"
   129→      {}
   130→    else
   131→      { "certResolver" => "letsencrypt" }
   132→    end
   133→  end
   134→
   135→  def delete_traefik_config(sandbox)
   136→    path = traefik_config_path(sandbox)
   137→    File.delete(path) if File.exist?(path)
   138→  end
   139→end
   140→
```

> AGENT

I see it! The flow:

1. POST `/sandboxes/3/terminal?type=tmux` → 303 redirect to `/terminal/3/tmux` ✓
2. GET `/terminal/auth` (forwardAuth) → 200 OK ✓ (twice — initial page + websocket)
3. Then later GET `/terminal/auth` → **302 redirect to login** 

The forwardAuth succeeds initially but the ttyd page actually loads! The question is what the browser shows. The terminal URL redirects to `https://100.106.185.92/terminal/3/tmux` which Traefik proxies to `thies-calm-tiger:7681` (ttyd). 

Let me check what the `SANDCASTLE_TLS_MODE` is on production — because the `HostRegexp(.+)` rule is used for selfsigned mode:

No `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL` or `SANDCASTLE_VNC_URL` set! Looking back at `TerminalController#terminal_redirect_url`:

```ruby
def terminal_redirect_url(path)
  base = ENV["SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL"]
  base ? "#{base}#{path}" : path
end
```

Without `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL`, it returns just the path `/terminal/3/tmux` — that's fine, it's a relative redirect.

> AGENT

**Found it!** The terminal/VNC routes are serving **Traefik's default self-signed cert** (`CN=TRAEFIK DEFAULT CERT`), NOT the actual cert at `/data/certs/cert.pem`. 

The issue: the `tls: {}` config on the terminal/VNC routers doesn't reference any cert store. The `HostRegexp(.+)` rule means Traefik can't match it to the TLS cert configured in `tls.yml` (which is likely keyed to the IP).

Let me check the TLS config:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_013HDnPH4UPKzk3di8T629nS
```json
{
  "command": "ssh thies@100.106.185.92 \"sudo cat /sandcastle/data/traefik/dynamic/tls.yml\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Check TLS dynamic config"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_013HDnPH4UPKzk3di8T629nS
```
---
tls:
  certificates:
  - certFile: "/data/certs/cert.pem"
    keyFile: "/data/certs/key.pem"
```

> AGENT

The TLS config only lists certificates but doesn't set a **default certificate store**. When Traefik can't determine the SNI (because `HostRegexp(.+)` has no domain), it uses its auto-generated default cert instead of the configured one.

The fix: add a default certificate store to the TLS config.

> TOOL

   156→

   159→      end

```

> AGENT

Line 161 — the TLS config only writes `certificates` but no `stores.default.defaultCertificate`. That's why Traefik uses its auto-generated cert for routes without explicit SNI matching.

The fix: add a default certificate store so Traefik uses our cert for all TLS connections.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_015SEo36iDkvKKR3u41TkAsE
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/route_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "      File.write(tls_path, { \"tls\" => { \"certificates\" => certs } }.to_yaml)",
  "new_string": "      tls_config = {\n        \"tls\" => {\n          \"certificates\" => certs,\n          \"stores\" => {\n            \"default\" => {\n              \"defaultCertificate\" => {\n                \"certFile\" => \"#{cert_dir}/cert.pem\",\n                \"keyFile\" => \"#{cert_dir}/key.pem\"\n              }\n            }\n          }\n        }\n      }\n      File.write(tls_path, tls_config.to_yaml)"
}
```

> TOOL

tool_result
id: toolu_012jVqev2wxVHyL1iAJNBVRY
```
   712→  end
   713→
   714→  # Pick an image guaranteed to be on this Docker daemon.
   715→  # Prefer busybox (tiny), fall back to alpine, then any local image.
   716→  def fix_image
   717→    %w[busybox:latest alpine:latest].each do |img|
   718→      return img if Docker::Image.get(img) rescue nil
   719→    end
   720→    # Last resort: use any image already present
   721→    all = Docker::Image.all
   722→    raise Error, "No local images available for docker_run_fix" if all.empty?
   723→    tags = all.first.info["RepoTags"]
   724→    tags&.first || all.first.id
   725→  end
   726→
```

> AGENT

The issue is operator precedence: `return img if Docker::Image.get(img) rescue nil` — the `rescue nil` applies to the entire `return img if ...` expression. When `Docker::Image.get` succeeds, it returns a truthy object, but `rescue nil` catches the whole thing oddly. Let me fix the precedence:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_01MiXSKyP5PBruXxNXQTHEFg
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/sandbox_manager.rb",
  "old_string": "    %w[busybox:latest alpine:latest].each do |img|\n      return img if Docker::Image.get(img) rescue nil\n    end",
  "new_string": "    %w[busybox:latest alpine:latest].each do |img|\n      begin\n        return img if Docker::Image.get(img)\n      rescue Docker::Error::DockerError\n        next\n      end\n    end"
}
```

> AGENT

**Root cause:** Traefik was serving its auto-generated `CN=TRAEFIK DEFAULT CERT` for terminal/VNC routes because the `HostRegexp(.+)` rule has no SNI, and there was no default certificate store configured. 

**Fix:** Added `stores.default.defaultCertificate` to the TLS config pointing to the actual cert. Production is already hotpatched — the browser should work now. The code fix will take effect on next deploy.

> AGENT

That's from my debug command earlier — the `rails runner` tried to `File.read` the tls.yml after `write_tls_config` deleted it (old code path). It's a one-time occurrence, not a recurring issue. You can safely resolve it in SolidErrors.

> AGENT

The issue is in the `rescue ActiveRecord::RecordNotFound` and `rescue Docker::Error::DockerError` blocks — they render a partial without specifying format, so when the request comes in as JSON (from polling), it fails because there's no JSON partial.

These rescue blocks need to respect the response format:

> TOOL

> AGENT

The admin stats endpoint only renders HTML partials (no `respond_to` block), so it's only called as HTML — no JSON format issue there. The fix for the dashboard controller is sufficient.

Fixed `DashboardController#stats` — the `rescue` blocks now use `respond_to` to return JSON when the request format is JSON, preventing the `MissingTemplate` error for `.json` requests to destroyed sandboxes.

> AGENT

The problem is clear: both engines hardcode `layout "solid_errors/application"` and `layout "mission_control/jobs/application"` in their class bodies. In development mode with code reloading, the class body re-executes and overrides the `after_initialize` setting.

The fix is to override the engine layout files in the app's views directory — Rails looks in app views first.

   157→  Prevent `sub` and `sup` elements from affecting the line height in all browsers.
   158→  */
   159→
   160→  sub,
   161→  sup {
   162→    font-size: 75%;
   163→    line-height: 0;
   164→    position: relative;
   165→    vertical-align: baseline;
   166→  }
   167→
   168→  sub {
   169→    bottom: -0.25em;
   170→  }
   171→
   172→  sup {
   173→    top: -0.5em;
   174→  }
   175→
   176→  /*
   177→  1. Remove text indentation from table contents in Chrome and Safari. (https://bugs.chromium.org/p/chromium/issues/detail?id=999088, https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=201297)
   178→  2. Correct table border color inheritance in all Chrome and Safari. (https://bugs.chromium.org/p/chromium/issues/detail?id=935729, https://bugs.webkit.org/show_bug.cgi?id=195016)
   179→  3. Remove gaps between table borders by default.
   180→  */
   181→
   182→  table {
   183→    text-indent: 0;
   184→    /* 1 */
   185→    border-color: inherit;
   186→    /* 2 */
   187→    border-collapse: collapse;
   188→    /* 3 */
   189→  }
   190→
   191→  /*
   192→  1. Change the font styles in all browsers.
   193→  2. Remove the margin in Firefox and Safari.
   194→  3. Remove default padding in all browsers.
   195→  */
   196→
   197→  button,
   198→  input,
   199→  optgroup,
   200→  select,
   201→  textarea {
   202→    font-family: inherit;
   203→    /* 1 */
   204→    font-feature-settings: inherit;
   205→    /* 1 */
   206→    font-variation-settings: inherit;
   207→    /* 1 */
   208→    font-size: 100%;
   209→    /* 1 */
   210→    font-weight: inherit;
   211→    /* 1 */
   212→    line-height: inherit;
   213→    /* 1 */
   214→    color: inherit;
   215→    /* 1 */
   216→    margin: 0;
   217→    /* 2 */
   218→    padding: 0;
   219→    /* 3 */
   220→  }
   221→
   222→  /*
   223→  Remove the inheritance of text transform in Edge and Firefox.
   224→  */
   225→
   226→  button,
   227→  select {
   228→    text-transform: none;
   229→  }
   230→
   231→  /*
   232→  1. Correct the inability to style clickable types in iOS and Safari.
   233→  2. Remove default button styles.
   234→  */
   235→
   236→  button,
   237→  [type='button'],
   238→  [type='reset'],
   239→  [type='submit'] {
   240→    -webkit-appearance: button;
   241→    /* 1 */
   242→    background-color: transparent;
   243→    /* 2 */
   244→    background-image: none;
   245→    /* 2 */
   246→  }
   247→
   248→  /*
   249→  Use the modern Firefox focus style for all focusable elements.
   250→  */
   251→
   252→  :-moz-focusring {
   253→    outline: auto;
   254→  }
   255→
   256→  /*
   257→  Remove the additional `:invalid` styles in Firefox. (https://github.com/mozilla/gecko-dev/blob/2f9eacd9d3d995c937b4251a5557d95d494c9be1/layout/style/res/forms.css#L728-L737)
   258→  */
   259→
   260→  :-moz-ui-invalid {
   261→    box-shadow: none;
   262→  }
   263→
   264→  /*
   265→  Add the correct vertical alignment in Chrome and Firefox.
   266→  */
   267→
   268→  progress {
   269→    vertical-align: baseline;
   270→  }
   271→
   272→  /*
   273→  Correct the cursor style of increment and decrement buttons in Safari.
   274→  */
   275→
   276→  ::-webkit-inner-spin-button,
   277→  ::-webkit-outer-spin-button {
   278→    height: auto;
   279→  }
   280→
   281→  /*
   282→  1. Correct the odd appearance in Chrome and Safari.
   283→  2. Correct the outline style in Safari.
   284→  */
   285→
   286→  [type='search'] {
   287→    -webkit-appearance: textfield;
   288→    /* 1 */
   289→    outline-offset: -2px;
   290→    /* 2 */
   291→  }
   292→
   293→  /*
   294→  Remove the inner padding in Chrome and Safari on macOS.
   295→  */
   296→
   297→  ::-webkit-search-decoration {
   298→    -webkit-appearance: none;
   299→  }
   300→
   301→  /*
   302→  1. Correct the inability to style clickable types in iOS and Safari.
   303→  2. Change font properties to `inherit` in Safari.
   304→  */
   305→
   306→  ::-webkit-file-upload-button {
   307→    -webkit-appearance: button;
   308→    /* 1 */
   309→    font: inherit;
   310→    /* 2 */
   311→  }
   312→
   313→  /*
   314→  Add the correct display in Chrome and Safari.
   315→  */
   316→
   317→  summary {
   318→    display: list-item;
   319→  }
   320→
   321→  /*
   322→  Removes the default spacing and border for appropriate elements.
   323→  */
   324→
   325→  blockquote,
   326→  dl,
   327→  dd,
   328→  h1,
   329→  h2,
   330→  h3,
   331→  h4,
   332→  h5,
   333→  h6,
   334→  hr,
   335→  figure,
   336→  p,
   337→  pre {
   338→    margin: 0;
   339→  }
   340→
   341→  fieldset {
   342→    margin: 0;
   343→    padding: 0;
   344→  }
   345→
   346→  legend {
   347→    padding: 0;
   348→  }
   349→
   350→  ol,
   351→  ul,
   352→  menu {
   353→    list-style: none;
   354→    margin: 0;
   355→    padding: 0;
   356→  }
   357→
   358→  /*
   359→  Reset default styling for dialogs.
   360→  */
   361→
   362→  dialog {
   363→    padding: 0;
   364→  }
   365→
   366→  /*
   367→  Prevent resizing textareas horizontally by default.
   368→  */
   369→
   370→  textarea {
   371→    resize: vertical;
   372→  }
   373→
   374→  /*
   375→  1. Reset the default placeholder opacity in Firefox. (https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/issues/3300)
   376→  2. Set the default placeholder color to the user's configured gray 400 color.
   377→  */
   378→
   379→  input::placeholder,
   380→  textarea::placeholder {
   381→    opacity: 1;
   382→    /* 1 */
   383→    color: #9ca3af;
   384→    /* 2 */
   385→  }
   386→
   387→  /*
   388→  Set the default cursor for buttons.
   389→  */
   390→
   391→  button,
   392→  [role="button"] {
   393→    cursor: pointer;
   394→  }
   395→
   396→  /*
   397→  Make sure disabled buttons don't get the pointer cursor.
   398→  */
   399→
   400→  :disabled {
   401→    cursor: default;
   402→  }
   403→
   404→  /*
   405→  1. Make replaced elements `display: block` by default. (https://github.com/mozdevs/cssremedy/issues/14)
   406→  2. Add `vertical-align: middle` to align replaced elements more sensibly by default. (https://github.com/jensimmons/cssremedy/issues/14#issuecomment-634934210)
   407→    This can trigger a poorly considered lint error in some tools but is included by design.
   408→  */
   409→
   410→  img,
   411→  svg,
   412→  video,
   413→  canvas,
   414→  audio,
   415→  iframe,
   416→  embed,
   417→  object {
   418→    display: block;
   419→    /* 1 */
   420→    vertical-align: middle;
   421→    /* 2 */
   422→  }
   423→
   424→  /*
   425→  Constrain images and videos to the parent width and preserve their intrinsic aspect ratio. (https://github.com/mozdevs/cssremedy/issues/14)
   426→  */
   427→
   428→  img,
   429→  video {
   430→    max-width: 100%;
   431→    height: auto;
   432→  }
   433→
   434→  /* Make elements with the HTML hidden attribute stay hidden by default */
   435→
   436→  [hidden] {
   437→    display: none;
   438→  }

> AGENT

SolidErrors bundles its own Tailwind v3 CSS inline. That'll conflict with our Tailwind v4. Better to scope it. Let me also check what the admin layout's `<%= yield %>` does — since we're using `render template:` with a block, the block becomes the content that replaces the `<%= yield %>` in the admin layout.

Let me also add the SolidErrors inline styles to the `<head>` section via `content_for`:

> TOOL

```json
{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/solid_errors/application.html.erb",
  "content": "<% content_for :head do %>\n  <%= render \"layouts/solid_errors/style\" %>\n<% end %>\n\n<%= render template: \"layouts/admin\" do %>\n  <div class=\"max-w-6xl mx-auto\">\n    <%= content_for?(:content) ? yield(:content) : yield %>\n  </div>\n<% end %>\n"
}

> AGENT

Everything looks good. The layout overrides will now:

1. **SolidErrors** — renders inside the admin layout with navbar, subnav, flash, footer. Injects SolidErrors' own CSS via `content_for :head`.
2. **MissionControl::Jobs** — renders inside the admin layout. Bulma CSS/JS is already loaded conditionally in the admin layout for `/admin/jobs` paths.

The key difference from the previous approach: instead of trying to override `layout "admin"` on the controller (which gets clobbered by the engine's class body on reload), we override the engine's layout *view file* — Rails resolves `app/views/` before engine views, so our files always win.

Done. The engines will now render inside the admin layout with the navbar, subnav tabs, flash messages, and footer — just like every other admin page.

The proper nested layout pattern in Rails: use `content_for` to capture the engine content, then yield it in the parent layout.

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_019aa9S5oPvqCh2YKKDfv4MS
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/admin.html.erb",
  "old_string": "      <%= yield %>",
  "new_string": "      <%= content_for?(:content) ? yield(:content) : yield %>"
}
```

> AGENT

Here's a summary of the fixes:

**Layout overrides** (the correct nested layout pattern):
- Engine layout views at `app/views/layouts/solid_errors/application.html.erb` and `app/views/layouts/mission_control/jobs/application.html.erb` override the engine's own layouts (Rails resolves app views before engine views)
- They use `content_for :content` to capture engine content, then `render template: "layouts/admin"` to wrap it in the admin layout
- Admin layout now uses `content_for?(:content) ? yield(:content) : yield` so it works both normally and with nested layouts

**`solid_errors` table**: The database exists but the schema hasn't been loaded on production. It'll be created on next deploy when `db:prepare` runs. You can also run it manually: `docker exec sandcastle-web ./bin/rails db:prepare`

Cache and cable don't have `migrations_paths` dirs at all — they just use schema files. The `errors` database has an empty `errors_migrate/` dir and `migrations_paths` configured but no `schema_dump` pointing to the right file. That's why `db:prepare` skips the schema load for errors.

The `schema_dump: errors_schema` I added should fix future deploys. The tables are now created in dev. Reload `/admin/errors` — it should work.

Now the tables were loaded into the primary DB earlier by my runner command. They need to be in the errors DB. Let me load the schema into the correct database:

> TOOL

tool_use Bash
id: toolu_01FW9Q1CBz81GngKXUJePX4r
```json
{
  "command": "docker exec sandcastle-web ./bin/rails runner \"\n  config = ActiveRecord::Base.configurations.configs_for(env_name: 'development', name: 'errors')\n  conn = ActiveRecord::Base.postgresql_connection(config.configuration_hash)\n  conn.execute('CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS solid_errors (id bigserial PRIMARY KEY, exception_class text NOT NULL, message text NOT NULL, severity text NOT NULL, source text, resolved_at timestamp, fingerprint varchar(64) NOT NULL, created_at timestamp NOT NULL, updated_at timestamp NOT NULL)')\n  conn.execute('CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS index_solid_errors_on_fingerprint ON solid_errors (fingerprint)')\n  conn.execute('CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS index_solid_errors_on_resolved_at ON solid_errors (resolved_at)')\n  conn.execute('CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS solid_errors_occurrences (id bigserial PRIMARY KEY, error_id integer NOT NULL, backtrace text, context json, created_at timestamp NOT NULL, updated_at timestamp NOT NULL)')\n  conn.execute('CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS index_solid_errors_occurrences_on_error_id ON solid_errors_occurrences (error_id)')\n  conn.execute('ALTER TABLE solid_errors_occurrences DROP CONSTRAINT IF EXISTS fk_rails_solid_errors_occurrences_error_id')\n  conn.execute('ALTER TABLE solid_errors_occurrences ADD CONSTRAINT fk_rails_solid_errors_occurrences_error_id FOREIGN KEY (error_id) REFERENCES solid_errors(id)')\n  puts 'Done. Tables: ' + conn.tables.inspect\n  conn.close\n\" 2>&1",
  "description": "Create solid_errors tables directly in the errors database",
  "timeout": 15000
}
```

> AGENT

Fixed. The issue was two things:

1. **Missing `connects_to`** in `development.rb` — SolidErrors was querying the primary database instead of the `errors` database
2. **Tables not created** in the errors database — `db:prepare` didn't load the schema because it wasn't wired up

Both are fixed now. Reload `/admin/errors`.

Now I have all the information needed. Here is my analysis and recommendation.

---

## Research Findings and Recommendation

### Current State in Sandcastle

The app already has the right architectural pieces in place:

- **`/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/config/initializers/mission_control_jobs.rb`** sets `base_controller_class` to `"Admin::BaseController"` and disables HTTP basic auth.
- **`/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/mission_control/jobs/application.html.erb`** overrides the gem's layout by placing a file at the same path. It uses `content_for :head` and `content_for :content` to embed MissionControl inside the admin layout (same pattern used by Solid Errors at `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/solid_errors/application.html.erb`).

The problem is on **line 2** of the custom layout override:

```erb
<%= stylesheet_link_tag "mission_control/jobs/bulma.min" %>
```

This loads Bulma's full 21,000-line CSS into the `<head>` of the admin layout, which **also** loads Tailwind via `<%= stylesheet_link_tag :app %>`. Bulma v1.0.2 sets aggressive global styles on `:root`, `body`, `html`, `section`, `h1`-`h6`, `table`, `button`, etc., which clash directly with Tailwind's preflight/reset and utility classes. The "big white M" is likely Bulma's `.hero` or `.section` styling combined with its default `background-color` and font settings overriding Tailwind's.

### The Gem's Layout Architecture

From examining the gem at `/Users/thies/.local/share/mise/installs/ruby/4.0.0/lib/ruby/gems/4.0.0/gems/mission_control-jobs-1.1.0/`:

- The gem's layout (`app/views/layouts/mission_control/jobs/application.html.erb`) is a full `<!DOCTYPE html>` document with its own `<head>` and `<body>`.
- It loads **two CSS files**: `bulma.min.css` (the full Bulma framework) and `application.css` (small custom styles for forms and job tables).
- It loads JS via its **own importmap** (Turbo, Stimulus, plus a form controller and debounce helpers).
- All views use Bulma CSS classes (`is-boxed`, `section`, `container`, `tabs`, `table`, `button`, `is-active`, etc.).

### Available Solutions (Ranked)

#### Option 1: CSS `@layer` Isolation (Recommended)

Wrap Bulma in a low-priority CSS cascade layer so Tailwind always wins outside the MissionControl content area. This is the cleanest approach because it requires no iframe, no Shadow DOM, and no rewriting of MissionControl's views.

**How it works:**

1. Create a custom CSS file (e.g., `app/assets/stylesheets/mission_control_scoped.css`) that wraps Bulma in a layer:

```css
@layer bulma {
  @import "mission_control/jobs/bulma.min";
  @import "mission_control/jobs/application";
}
```

2. In your layout override, replace the two `stylesheet_link_tag` calls with the single scoped file, and add a scoping wrapper `<div>`:

```erb
<% content_for :head do %>
  <%= stylesheet_link_tag "mission_control_scoped", "data-turbo-track": "reload" %>
  <%= javascript_importmap_tags "application", importmap: MissionControl::Jobs.importmap %>
<% end %>

<% content_for :content do %>
  <div class="mc-jobs max-w-6xl mx-auto">
    <%= render "layouts/mission_control/jobs/flash" %>
    <%= render "layouts/mission_control/jobs/navigation" %>
    <%= yield %>
  </div>
<% end %>

<%= render template: "layouts/admin" %>
```

3. Since Tailwind v4 already uses `@layer` internally (base, components, utilities), and unlayered CSS beats layered CSS in the cascade, Tailwind's styles will take precedence globally. To make Bulma win **inside** the MissionControl area, add specificity via the `.mc-jobs` wrapper:

```css
@layer bulma {
  .mc-jobs {
    /* Bulma imports here, or re-scope key Bulma styles */
  }
}
```

**Caveat:** The `@layer` approach works well if your asset pipeline supports `@import` inside `@layer`. With Propshaft (which Sandcastle likely uses given Rails 8.1), CSS `@import` is not processed -- files are served as-is. So you would need to either:
- Concatenate the Bulma CSS into your layer file at build time, or
- Use the Tailwind CSS `@layer` approach differently.

**Practical Propshaft-compatible version:** Instead of CSS `@import`, create a wrapper file that uses a descendant selector to scope Bulma. You would need to preprocess Bulma to wrap all its rules under `.mc-jobs`. A one-time build step:

```bash
# Wrap bulma in a scoping selector
echo '.mc-jobs {' > app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
cat path/to/bulma.min.css >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
echo '}' >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
```

This is crude but effective -- all Bulma selectors become `.mc-jobs :root`, `.mc-jobs body`, `.mc-jobs .section`, etc., which limits their scope. The `:root` and `body` selectors won't match inside a div, effectively neutering Bulma's global resets.

#### Option 2: Iframe Isolation (Simplest, Most Bulletproof)

Serve MissionControl in an `<iframe>` so it gets its own document context. Bulma and Tailwind never see each other.

1. Create a dedicated route that renders MissionControl's original full-page layout (with Bulma in `<head>`).
2. In the admin layout, embed it:

```erb
<% content_for :content do %>
  <iframe src="<%= mission_control_jobs_path %>" class="w-full min-h-screen border-0"></iframe>
<% end %>
<%= render template: "layouts/admin" %>
```

**Pros:** Zero CSS conflicts guaranteed.
**Cons:** Two separate documents means no shared Turbo navigation, auth requires the iframe source to be on the same origin, and resizing/scrolling can be awkward.

#### Option 3: Scope Bulma via Nesting with PostCSS or `postcss-prefix-selector`

If you add a build step (e.g., via `cssbundling-rails`), use a PostCSS plugin to automatically prefix every Bulma selector with `.mc-jobs`. This is the automated version of Option 1's manual approach.

### Concrete Recommendation

**Go with a scoped Bulma CSS file (variant of Option 1).** Here is the specific plan:

1. **Generate a scoped Bulma file** by nesting the entire Bulma CSS under a `.mc-jobs` selector. You only need to do this once (and re-do it when upgrading mission_control-jobs). The MissionControl gem's `bulma.min.css` and custom CSS files are small and stable:

```bash
cd /Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle
echo '.mc-jobs {' > app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
cat ~/.local/share/mise/installs/ruby/4.0.0/lib/ruby/gems/4.0.0/gems/mission_control-jobs-1.1.0/app/assets/stylesheets/mission_control/jobs/bulma.min.css >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
cat ~/.local/share/mise/installs/ruby/4.0.0/lib/ruby/gems/4.0.0/gems/mission_control-jobs-1.1.0/app/assets/stylesheets/mission_control/jobs/forms.css >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css  
cat ~/.local/share/mise/installs/ruby/4.0.0/lib/ruby/gems/4.0.0/gems/mission_control-jobs-1.1.0/app/assets/stylesheets/mission_control/jobs/jobs.css >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
echo '}' >> app/assets/stylesheets/bulma_scoped.css
```

2. **Update the layout override** at `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/mission_control/jobs/application.html.erb`:

```erb
<% content_for :head do %>
  <%= stylesheet_link_tag "bulma_scoped", "data-turbo-track": "reload" %>
  <%= javascript_importmap_tags "application", importmap: MissionControl::Jobs.importmap %>
<% end %>

<% content_for :content do %>
  <div class="mc-jobs max-w-6xl mx-auto">
    <%= render "layouts/mission_control/jobs/flash" %>
    <%= render "layouts/mission_control/jobs/navigation" %>
    <%= yield %>
  </div>
<% end %>

<%= render template: "layouts/admin" %>
```

3. **Remove the original Bulma/application stylesheet_link_tags** (lines 2-3 of the current file) -- they are replaced by the single `bulma_scoped` link.

This approach:
- Keeps Bulma's styles scoped to the `.mc-jobs` container div
- Prevents Bulma's `:root` and `body` global styles from affecting the rest of the page (they become `.mc-jobs :root` and `.mc-jobs body` which won't match anything)
- Preserves all of MissionControl's internal styling (tabs, tables, buttons all still work because they are descendants of `.mc-jobs`)
- Requires no build tooling changes -- just a static CSS file served by Propshaft
- Only needs regeneration when upgrading the mission_control-jobs gem version

Sources:
- [mission_control-jobs GitHub repo](https://github.com/rails/mission_control-jobs)
- [mission_control-jobs README](https://github.com/rails/mission_control-jobs/blob/main/README.md)
- [CSS bundling support issue #164](https://github.com/rails/mission_control-jobs/issues/164)
- [How to use Tailwind with any CSS framework](https://dev.to/climentea/how-to-use-tailwind-with-any-css-framework-lca)
- [Bulma with Tailwind discussion](https://github.com/tailwindlabs/tailwindcss/discussions/2117)
- [Using CSS Cascade Layers with Tailwind](https://css-tricks.com/using-css-cascade-layers-to-manage-custom-styles-in-a-tailwind-project/)

{"body":"## Summary\n- Replace ttyd's built-in xterm.js frontend with [ghostty-web](https://github.com/coder/ghostty-web), bringing Ghostty's WASM terminal parser to the browser\n- Rails now serves its own terminal page (`/terminals/:id/:type`) with a Stimulus controller that bridges ghostty-web to ttyd's binary WebSocket protocol\n- ttyd continues running inside sandbox containers as the WebSocket/PTY backend — only the browser frontend changes\n\n## Changes\n- **ghostty-web@0.4.0** pinned via jsDelivr CDN in importmap\n- **Fullscreen terminal layout** (`layouts/terminal.html.erb`) — no navbar/footer\n- **Stimulus controller** (`ghostty_terminal_controller.js`) — handles ghostty-web init, ttyd binary protocol (input/output/resize), WebSocket reconnection, and terminal fitting\n- **`terminal#show`** action serves the new page; `terminal#open` redirects there instead of to ttyd's HTML\n- Tests updated for new redirect paths\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] Click tmux/shell button on a running sandbox — should open ghostty-web terminal in new tab\n- [ ] Verify terminal renders output correctly (colors, cursor, scrollback)\n- [ ] Verify keyboard input works (typing, ctrl sequences, arrow keys)\n- [ ] Verify terminal resizes when browser window is resized\n- [ ] Verify WebSocket reconnects on connection drop\n- [ ] Verify auth still works (unauthenticated users redirected to login)\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\n\n<!-- This is an auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->\n\n## Summary by CodeRabbit\n\n* **New Features**\n  * Added a web-based terminal interface for interacting with sandbox environments. Users can now access both tmux and shell terminal types directly in the browser with real-time input and output.\n\n<!-- end of auto-generated comment: release notes by coderabbit.ai -->","headRefName":"ghostty-web-terminal","title":"Replace ttyd frontend with ghostty-web terminal emulator"}

```
     1→import { Controller } from "@hotwired/stimulus"
     2→import { init, Terminal } from "ghostty-web"
     3→
     4→// Bridges ghostty-web terminal with ttyd's WebSocket protocol.
     5→//
     6→// ttyd protocol:
     7→//   Server→Client: first byte = command ('0'=output, '1'=title, '2'=preferences), rest = data
     8→//   Client→Server: first byte = command ('0'=input, '1'=resize JSON), rest = data
     9→//   On open: send JSON { AuthToken, columns, rows }
    10→//   WebSocket subprotocol: "tty"
    11→export default class extends Controller {
    12→  static values = { wsUrl: String, tokenUrl: String }
    13→
    14→  async connect() {
    15→    await init()
    16→
    17→    this.encoder = new TextEncoder()
    18→    this.decoder = new TextDecoder()
    19→
    20→    this.terminal = new Terminal({
    21→      fontSize: 14,
    22→      theme: {
    23→        background: "#1a1b26",
    24→        foreground: "#a9b1d6",
    25→      },
    26→    })
    27→
    28→    this.terminal.open(this.element)
    29→
    30→    this.terminal.onData((data) => this.sendInput(data))
    31→    this.terminal.onResize?.(({ cols, rows }) => this.sendResize(cols, rows))
    32→
    33→    this.token = await this.fetchToken()
    34→    this.connectWebSocket()
    35→
    36→    this.resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(() => this.fitTerminal())
    37→    this.resizeObserver.observe(this.element)
    38→  }
    39→
    40→  disconnect() {
    41→    this.resizeObserver?.disconnect()
    42→    this.socket?.close()
    43→    this.terminal?.dispose?.()
    44→  }
    45→
    46→  async fetchToken() {
    47→    try {
    48→      const resp = await fetch(this.tokenUrlValue)
    49→      if (resp.ok) {
    50→        const json = await resp.json()
    51→        return json.token
    52→      }
    53→    } catch (e) {
    54→      console.warn("[ghostty] token fetch failed:", e)
    55→    }
    56→    return ""
    57→  }
    58→
    59→  connectWebSocket() {
    60→    this.socket = new WebSocket(this.wsUrlValue, ["tty"])
    61→    this.socket.binaryType = "arraybuffer"
    62→
    63→    this.socket.addEventListener("open", () => {
    64→      const msg = JSON.stringify({
    65→        AuthToken: this.token,
    66→        columns: this.terminal.cols || 80,
    67→        rows: this.terminal.rows || 24,
    68→      })
    69→      this.socket.send(this.encoder.encode(msg))
    70→      this.terminal.focus?.()
    71→    })
    72→
    73→    this.socket.addEventListener("message", (event) => {
    74→      const rawData = event.data
    75→      const cmd = String.fromCharCode(new Uint8Array(rawData)[0])
    76→      const data = rawData.slice(1)
    77→
    78→      switch (cmd) {
    79→        case "0": // OUTPUT
    80→          this.terminal.write(new Uint8Array(data))
    81→          break
    82→        case "1": // SET_WINDOW_TITLE
    83→          document.title = this.decoder.decode(data) + " — Sandcastle"
    84→          break
    85→        case "2": // SET_PREFERENCES (ignored — we use our own theme)
    86→          break
    87→      }
    88→    })
    89→
    90→    this.socket.addEventListener("close", (event) => {
    91→      if (event.code !== 1000) {
    92→        console.log("[ghostty] connection lost, reconnecting...")
    93→        setTimeout(() => this.connectWebSocket(), 2000)
    94→      }
    95→    })
    96→  }
    97→
    98→  sendInput(data) {
    99→    if (this.socket?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return
   100→
   101→    if (typeof data === "string") {
   102→      const payload = new Uint8Array(data.length * 3 + 1)
   103→      payload[0] = "0".charCodeAt(0)
   104→      const stats = this.encoder.encodeInto(data, payload.subarray(1))
   105→      this.socket.send(payload.subarray(0, stats.written + 1))
   106→    } else {
   107→      const payload = new Uint8Array(data.length + 1)
   108→      payload[0] = "0".charCodeAt(0)
   109→      payload.set(data, 1)
   110→      this.socket.send(payload)
   111→    }
   112→  }
   113→
   114→  sendResize(cols, rows) {
   115→    if (this.socket?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return
   116→    const msg = JSON.stringify({ columns: cols, rows: rows })
   117→    this.socket.send(this.encoder.encode("1" + msg))
   118→  }
   119→
   120→  fitTerminal() {
   121→    this.terminal.resize?.(
   122→      Math.floor(this.element.clientWidth / 9),
   123→      Math.floor(this.element.clientHeight / 17)
   124→    )
   125→  }
   126→}
   127→
```

> AGENT

Found it. `SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL` is set to `https://dev.sand:8443` (a full URL), and then it's concatenated with `"#{proto}//#{host}"` which is also `wss://dev.sand:8443`. So the base URL gets doubled.

The `base` variable should only contain a path prefix, not a full URL. Let me fix it:

> TOOL

tool_use Edit
id: toolu_017Zi8n8PJwXVsVa6bEg8hEn
```json
{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb",
  "old_string": "    proto = request.ssl? ? \"wss:\" : \"ws:\"\n    host  = request.host_with_port\n    base  = ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL\"] || \"\"\n\n    @ws_url    = \"#{proto}//#{host}#{base}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/ws\"\n    @token_url = \"#{request.protocol}#{host}#{base}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/token\"",
  "new_string": "    terminal_base = ENV[\"SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL\"].presence\n    if terminal_base\n      # SANDCASTLE_TERMINAL_URL is a full URL (e.g. https://dev.sand:8443)\n      uri = URI.parse(terminal_base)\n      ws_proto = uri.scheme == \"https\" ? \"wss\" : \"ws\"\n      @ws_url    = \"#{ws_proto}://#{uri.host}:#{uri.port}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/ws\"\n      @token_url = \"#{uri.scheme}://#{uri.host}:#{uri.port}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/token\"\n    else\n      proto = request.ssl? ? \"wss:\" : \"ws:\"\n      host  = request.host_with_port\n      @ws_url    = \"#{proto}//#{host}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/ws\"\n      @token_url = \"#{request.protocol}#{host}/terminal/#{@sandbox.id}/#{type}/token\"\n    end"
}

Perfect! Now let me compile my findings into a comprehensive summary.

## Summary: Codebase Exploration

I've thoroughly explored the Sandcastle codebase to understand the User model, terminal system, and settings pages. Here are my findings:

### 1. User Model & Database Schema

**File**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/models/user.rb` and `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/db/schema.rb`

**User columns (from schema)**:
- `admin` (boolean, default: false)
- `chrome_persist_profile` (boolean, default: true) - Persists Chrome profile across sandbox recreations
- `email_address` (string, unique)
- `full_name` (string) - Used in .gitconfig
- `github_username` (string) - For git configuration
- `must_change_password` (boolean, default: false)
- `name` (string, unique) - Username (lowercase alphanumeric)
- `password_digest` (string)
- `sandbox_archive_retention_days` (integer, nullable) - Per-user override for archive retention
- `ssh_public_key` (text)
- `status` (string) - One of: 'active', 'suspended', 'pending_approval'
- `tailscale_auth_key` (string)
- `tailscale_auto_connect` (boolean, default: false)
- `tailscale_container_id` (string)
- `tailscale_network` (string)
- `tailscale_state` (string, default: 'disabled')
- `tailscale_subnet` (string)

**No terminal-specific preferences exist** - There are currently no user-level terminal settings (font size, theme, etc.) stored in the database.

### 2. Terminal System Architecture

**Terminal Controller**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb`

Main endpoints:
- `POST /terminal/:id/open` - Opens a terminal (creates WeTTY container or ttyd config)
- `GET /terminal/:id/:type` - Shows terminal view (type: "tmux" or "shell")
- `DELETE /terminal/:id/close` - Closes terminal
- `GET /terminal/auth` - ForwardAuth endpoint for Traefik validation

**Terminal View**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/terminal/show.html.erb`

Simple minimal view:
```erb
<div id="terminal-container"
     data-controller="ghostty-terminal"
     data-ghostty-terminal-ws-url-value="<%= @ws_url %>"
     data-ghostty-terminal-token-url-value="<%= @token_url %>">
</div>
```

### 3. Ghostty-Web Terminal (Current Implementation)

**File**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/javascript/controllers/ghostty_terminal_controller.js`

- Uses `ghostty-web` library (v0.4.0 from CDN)
- Communicates via WebSocket with ttyd protocol (subprotocol: "tty")
- Hard-coded theme: background `#1a1b26`, foreground `#a9b1d6`, fontSize 14px
- Handles three message types:
  - `0` = OUTPUT (terminal content)
  - `1` = SET_WINDOW_TITLE
  - `2` = SET_PREFERENCES (ignored, uses own theme)
- Auto-reconnects on WebSocket close (non-1000 codes)
- Resizes terminal based on element size

### 4. Terminal Manager (Backend Service)

**File**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/services/terminal_manager.rb`

Manages Traefik configurations for terminal access:
- Writes YAML configs to `/data/traefik/dynamic/terminal-{sandbox_id}.yml`
- Supports two ports: 7681 (tmux), 7682 (shell)
- Implements forwardAuth middleware for security
- Can be called before sandbox is running (`prepare_traefik_config`)
- Cleans up orphaned configs

### 5. Settings Page (User Preferences)

**File**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/settings/show.html.erb`

Current settings available:
- **Profile**: Full name, email, GitHub username
- **Password**: Change password
- **Tailscale**: Status display, auto-connect toggle
- **VNC**: Chrome profile persistence toggle
- **API Tokens**: Generate and manage API tokens
- **Sandbox Archive**: Set per-user retention period
- **SSH Keys**: Placeholder for future implementation (disabled/grayed out)

**Controller**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/controllers/settings_controller.rb`

The `profile_params` method permits: `:email_address`, `:full_name`, `:github_username`, `:chrome_persist_profile`, `:sandbox_archive_retention_days`

### 6. Terminal History: From WeTTY to ttyd to Ghostty-Web

**Git history shows**:
- Original: WeTTY sidecar containers (per-terminal ephemerals)
- Commit 4e5a4e9: **Replaced WeTTY with ttyd running inside sandboxes**
- Commit 09689be & 163d36d: **Replaced ttyd frontend with ghostty-web terminal emulator** (current state)
- Commit 344e320: Added xterm package to sandbox image

**No xterm.js code exists** - xterm.js was never integrated into the web UI. The package was added to the sandbox image for command-line use within terminals, but the web frontend has always used: WeTTY → ttyd → ghostty-web.

### 7. Layout & Styling

**Terminal Layout**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/views/layouts/terminal.html.erb`

Full-screen layout with:
- No padding/margins (0 on html/body)
- Dark background (#1a1b26)
- Full height/width terminal container
- Viewport meta tag for mobile
- Apple mobile web app capable

### 8. Import Map (Dependencies)

**File**: `/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/config/importmap.rb`

- `ghostty-web` pinned to v0.4.0 from CDN (jsdelivr)
- Uses Stimulus controllers for initialization
- Turbo for navigation

---

**Key Insight**: There are **no user preferences for terminal appearance/behavior** currently stored in the database. This would be the natural place to add customization options like font size, color themes, cursor style, etc., if needed in future.

{
  "file_path": "/Users/thies/Projects/GitHub/Sandcastle/app/javascript/controllers/xterm_terminal_controller.js",
  "content": "import { Controller } from \"@hotwired/stimulus\"\nimport { Terminal } from \"@xterm/xterm\"\nimport { FitAddon } from \"@xterm/addon-fit\"\nimport { WebLinksAddon } from \"@xterm/addon-web-links\"\n\n// Bridges xterm.js terminal with ttyd's WebSocket protocol.\n// Same protocol as ghostty_terminal_controller but using xterm.js as the renderer.\nexport default class extends Controller {\n  static values = { wsUrl: String, tokenUrl: String }\n\n  async connect() {\n    this.encoder = new TextEncoder()\n    this.decoder = new TextDecoder()\n\n    this.fitAddon = new FitAddon()\n\n    this.terminal = new Terminal({\n      fontSize: 14,\n      fontFamily: \"Menlo, Monaco, 'Courier New', monospace\",\n      cursorBlink: true,\n      theme: {\n        background: \"#1a1b26\",\n        foreground: \"#a9b1d6\",\n        cursor: \"#a9b1d6\",\n      },\n    })\n\n    this.terminal.loadAddon(this.fitAddon)\n    this.terminal.loadAddon(new WebLinksAddon())\n    this.terminal.open(this.element)\n    this.fitAddon.fit()\n\n    this.terminal.onData((data) => this.sendInput(data))\n    this.terminal.onBinary((data) => this.sendBinary(data))\n    this.terminal.onResize(({ cols, rows }) => this.sendResize(cols, rows))\n\n    this.token = await this.fetchToken()\n    this.connectWebSocket()\n\n    this.resizeObserver = new ResizeObserver(() => {\n      try { this.fitAddon.fit() } catch {}\n    })\n    this.resizeObserver.observe(this.element)\n  }\n\n  disconnect() {\n    this.resizeObserver?.disconnect()\n    this.socket?.close()\n    this.terminal?.dispose()\n  }\n\n  async fetchToken() {\n    try {\n      const resp = await fetch(this.tokenUrlValue)\n      if (resp.ok) {\n        const json = await resp.json()\n        return json.token\n      }\n    } catch (e) {\n      console.warn(\"[xterm] token fetch failed:\", e)\n    }\n    return \"\"\n  }\n\n  connectWebSocket() {\n    this.socket = new WebSocket(this.wsUrlValue, [\"tty\"])\n    this.socket.binaryType = \"arraybuffer\"\n\n    this.socket.addEventListener(\"open\", () => {\n      const msg = JSON.stringify({\n        AuthToken: this.token,\n        columns: this.terminal.cols,\n        rows: this.terminal.rows,\n      })\n      this.socket.send(this.encoder.encode(msg))\n      this.terminal.focus()\n    })\n\n    this.socket.addEventListener(\"message\", (event) => {\n      const rawData = event.data\n      const cmd = String.fromCharCode(new Uint8Array(rawData)[0])\n      const data = rawData.slice(1)\n\n      switch (cmd) {\n        case \"0\": // OUTPUT\n          this.terminal.write(new Uint8Array(data))\n          break\n        case \"1\": // SET_WINDOW_TITLE\n          document.title = this.decoder.decode(data) + \" — Sandcastle\"\n          break\n        case \"2\": // SET_PREFERENCES (ignored)\n          break\n      }\n    })\n\n    this.socket.addEventListener(\"close\", (event) => {\n      if (event.code !== 1000) {\n        console.log(\"[xterm] connection lost, reconnecting...\")\n        setTimeout(() => this.connectWebSocket(), 2000)\n      }\n    })\n  }\n\n  sendInput(data) {\n    if (this.socket?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return\n    const payload = new Uint8Array(data.length * 3 + 1)\n    payload[0] = \"0\".charCodeAt(0)\n    const stats = this.encoder.encodeInto(data, payload.subarray(1))\n    this.socket.send(payload.subarray(0, stats.written + 1))\n  }\n\n  sendBinary(data) {\n    if (this.socket?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return\n    const bytes = new Uint8Array(data.length + 1)\n    bytes[0] = \"0\".charCodeAt(0)\n    for (let i = 0; i < data.length; i++) bytes[i + 1] = data.charCodeAt(i) & 255\n    this.socket.send(bytes)\n  }\n\n  sendResize(cols, rows) {\n    if (this.socket?.readyState !== WebSocket.OPEN) return\n    const msg = JSON.stringify({ columns: cols, rows: rows })\n    this.socket.send(this.encoder.encode(\"1\" + msg))\n  }\n}\n"
}

     1→class SettingsController < ApplicationController
     2→  def show
     3→    @user = Current.user
     4→    @api_tokens = @user.api_tokens.active.order(created_at: :desc)
     5→  end
     6→
     7→  def update_profile
     8→    @user = Current.user
     9→
    10→    if @user.update(profile_params)
    11→      redirect_to settings_path, notice: "Profile updated successfully."
    12→    else
    13→      render :show, status: :unprocessable_entity
    14→    end
    15→  end
    16→
    17→  def update_password
    18→    @user = Current.user
    19→
    20→    unless @user.authenticate(params[:current_password])
    21→      redirect_to settings_path, alert: "Current password is incorrect."
    22→      return
    23→    end
    24→
    25→    if @user.update(password_params)
    26→      redirect_to settings_path, notice: "Password changed successfully."
    27→    else
    28→      redirect_to settings_path, alert: @user.errors.full_messages.join(", ")
    29→    end
    30→  end
    31→
    32→  def toggle_tailscale
    33→    @user = Current.user
    34→
    35→    if @user.update(tailscale_auto_connect: !@user.tailscale_auto_connect)
    36→      status = @user.tailscale_auto_connect ? "enabled" : "disabled"
    37→      redirect_to settings_path, notice: "Tailscale auto-connect #{status}."
    38→    else
    39→      redirect_to settings_path, alert: "Failed to update Tailscale settings."
    40→    end
    41→  end
    42→
    43→  def generate_token
    44→    @user = Current.user
    45→
    46→    token_name = params[:name].presence || "Web UI Token"
    47→    token, raw_token = ApiToken.generate_for(@user, name: token_name)
    48→
    49→    flash[:api_token] = raw_token
    50→    redirect_to settings_path, notice: "API token '#{token_name}' generated. Make sure to copy it now - you won't be able to see it again!"
    51→  end
    52→
    53→  def revoke_token
    54→    @user = Current.user
    55→    token = @user.api_tokens.find(params[:id])
    56→    token_name = token.name
    57→    token.destroy!
    58→
    59→    redirect_to settings_path, notice: "API token '#{token_name}' revoked."
    60→  end
    61→
    62→  private
    63→
    64→  def profile_params
    65→    params.require(:user).permit(:email_address, :full_name, :github_username, :chrome_persist_profile, :sandbox_archive_retention_days)
    66→  end
    67→
    68→  def password_params
    69→    params.require(:user).permit(:password, :password_confirmation)
    70→  end
    71→end
    72→

> AGENT

Everything looks good. Here's a summary of the changes:

**New files:**
- `app/javascript/controllers/xterm_terminal_controller.js` — xterm.js Stimulus controller (same ttyd protocol as ghostty)
- `app/assets/stylesheets/xterm.css` — xterm.js CSS
- `db/migrate/..._add_terminal_emulator_to_users.rb` — adds `terminal_emulator` column (default: "ghostty")
- `vendor/javascript/@xterm--*.js` — vendored xterm.js packages

**Modified files:**
- `app/controllers/terminal_controller.rb` — sets `@emulator` from user preference, fixes doubled URL bug
- `app/views/terminal/show.html.erb` — switches between ghostty/xterm controller based on `@emulator`
- `app/views/layouts/terminal.html.erb` — conditionally loads xterm.css, fixes deprecated meta tag
- `app/models/user.rb` — validates `terminal_emulator` inclusion
- `app/controllers/settings_controller.rb` — permits `terminal_emulator` param
- `app/views/settings/show.html.erb` — adds Terminal section with dropdown
- `config/importmap.rb` — pins xterm.js packages

Users can now switch between Ghostty Web and xterm.js from Settings.
